CHAPTER 1
FUNDAMENTALS OF WEB DESIGN
Introduction:
Internet technology has developed tremendously over the years and still it is developing towards the betterment of mankind. Nowadays, each and every field has converted from conventional traditional method into online mode. It has turned the world into a huge virtual place where businessmen can conduct businesses, teachers can engage classes and farmers can use information and technology required for yielding good quality crops. No field is left out without the use of information and technology. Everywhere we use information required and the internet is the only place where you can find tons of information available. With the help of the internet we are able to buy anything online, this shrinks the geographic limitation of the product or service.
In the early days we had only standalone computers. The computers that are not connected with one another with the intention of data transfer are standalone computers. This restricts the user to use the computer by only one user at a time and when two people wanted to exchange information, they had to physically carry the data from one computer to another. This led to a new scope for the programmers to make two computers to communicate with each other to form a computer network.
A computer network is an interconnection of autonomous computers for the purpose of communication and data sharing. Two computers are said to be interconnected if they can exchange information. The connection can be through copper wires, lasers, satellite communication etc. irrespective of the distance where these computers are located. Therefore, the internet is a collection of computer networks all over the world. The Internet is a network of networks. Hundreds of computers are connected across hundreds of countries via telephone lines, fibre optic cables, satellite communication and so on. Every computer in the network is called a host. Each host in the network can be identified uniquely by what is called an IP (Internet Protocol) address. The IP address is mapped onto the Domain Name System (DNS) which is represented as www.gmail.com. The DNS is used instead of the IP address since it is difficult to remember numbers but the DNS is any logical address which can be easily remembered. The DNS is entered in the address in the address bar of any browser.
A web browser is an application software that enables a user to display an internet with the images, videos, music and other information located on a web page on the World Wide Web (WWW) and it is only a part of the internet.
Services on the internet: Information and technology is used everywhere. It is used in the field of science and technology, engineering, banking, business, agriculture, medicine, space science, nuclear research, education, aviation, multimedia etc. some of the services on the internet are listed below.
The World Wide Web (W3C) or World Wide Web Consortium:
The collection of web pages in all websites on the internet is called WWW. These web pages consist of information in the form of text, pictures, audio, animation and video formats.
e-mail:
Electronic mail is used to exchange messages from anywhere in the world. It is less expensive, faster and easy to use. There are a number of advantages to email.
-          Easy to use: As email manages the data of our contacts, sends mail quickly, maintains mail history etc. hence, it is very easy to use.
-          Speed: Within no time messages get exchanged from anywhere all across the globe. We can send the same message to more than one person at a time hence it saves a lot of time.
-          Easy to prioritize: The short information is present in the subject line of an email, hence mails can be easily prioritized and ignore unwanted mails.
-          Reliable and secure: it is a reliable and secure way to pass the information from one computer to another computer system over the internet.
-          Informal and conversational: messages can be exchanged between family and friends and have interaction in an informal way with the friends and family members. It is a good tool to interact with anyone.
-          Easier for reference: emails are used to reply to messages by attaching previous mails sent or received for reference purposes.
-          Automated emails: automated emails will be sent by the system automatically without human intervention. Once the program-like autoresponders are set, whenever we receive an email immediately an email will be sent to an email sender.
-          Environment friendly: traditional way of sending messages uses paper and a lot of fuel to travel and reach to its destination. Email prevents a lot of trees getting axed and saves fuel as it travels using electrical pulses.
-          Use of graphics: in the traditional way of sending messages lags graphics and other pictures but with an email we can send and receive beautiful graphics and images.
-          Advertising tool: email is used in email marketing to generate revenue for the individual or company to sell their products and services.
-          Economic: use of email is economic as it uses electricity and the internet connectivity.
-          Storage: An email service provider provides some space for storing the data and information for user reference. Emails can be sorted either in ascending or descending order. Emails can be sorted as per the date. This makes an email easier for users.
-          Provision of attachments: Users can send a huge amount of information in the email attachment. It is a tool used to send word documents, excel spreadsheet information etc. and it is not going to cost anything extra as in the postal service.
Disadvantages of email:
-          Viruses: Viruses are the malicious computer software that disrupt the normal functioning of the computer system. Some viruses copy themselves and further infect the computer. Email recipients have to scan the mails and they have the potential to harm computer systems.
-          Spam: unsolicited messages and unwanted advertisements create nuisance and are terminated as spam. It is necessary to block or filter unwanted emails by means of spam filters. Spamming is practiced by sending hoax emails. Email spoofing. It is another common practice, used for spamming.
-          Hacking: The act of breaking into computer security is termed as hacking. In this type of security breach, emails are intercepted by hackers. An email, before it is delivered to the recipient, bounces between servers located in different locations.
Email discussion group: These email discussion groups are used to discuss different topics with other people by email.
News groups: These are the discussion groups used to exchange information. A user sends a message to the news group and the other user reads this message and replies.
Ecommerce: electronic commerce is a way of carrying out business dealings by internet. E Commerce is a process in which the selling of goods, services between two partners is based on the protocol set. Protocol is a set of rules agreed between two trading partners. E Commerce includes online banking, online shopping and online education etc.
Some of the technologies and services used in e-commerce are,
-          Electronic Data Interchange (EDI): it is used to send information to another company electronically rather than with paper. Business entities which conduct business electronically are called trading partners.
-          Email
-          Electronic Fund Transfer (EFT)
-          Electronic Benefit Transfer (EBT)
-          Electronic forms (Online admission forms, Online Job application forms etc).
-          Digital Cash (DC)
-          Bulletin Boards (BBs)
-          Electronic Banking (EB)
-          Bar coding, imaging voice recognition
-          Security services such as firewalls, encryption, gateways etc.
Types of ecommerce:
Business to Business (B2B): The exchange of services, information and goods from one business to another business partners. Ex ebay.com
Business to Consumer (B2C): The exchange of services, information and goods from business to consumers. Ex: amazon.com, flipkart.com etc.
Consumer to Business (C2B): The exchange of services, information and goods from customer to business. Customers directly contact with business vendors by posting their project work online so that the needy companies review it and contact the customer directly with bid. The consumer reviews all the bids and selects the company for further processing. Ex guru.com, freelancer.com etc.
Consumer to consumer (C2C): The exchange of services, information and goods from one customer to another customer. Ex olx.com
Advantages of electronic commerce:
-          Easier entry to the new marketplace.
-          Easy entry to remote markets.
-          Establish a new market at an economical rate.
-          Global participation.
-          Optimization of resources.
-          Reduce time to complete business transactions.
-          Improved market intelligence and strategic planning.
FTP (File Transfer Protocol): This protocol helps to transfer files on the internet. Files containing audio, video, graphics and data files can be uploaded and downloaded using FTP.
Telnet: This program is used to access a remote computer on the internet. You can access data and files from a connected computer by telnet.
Chat: This is the service of the internet using which two or more users can live chat with each other. We can chat by text or audio or video.
Gopher: A gopher on the internet is a system for users to search for files via menus or directory structures. It uses plain English names and is text based only.
Some terms related to web:
WWW (World Wide Web): WWW was founded by Tim Berners Lee in September 1994 at Massachusetts institute of technology with support from Defence Advanced Research Projects Agency (DARPA) and the European commission. WWW is a set of protocols that allow you to access any document on the net through a naming system based on URLs. WWW also specifies a way – the HyperText Transfer Protocol (HTTP) to request and send a document over the internet.
Web page: A web page is a web document that can be accessed through a web browser and displayed on a monitor or mobile device. This information is in HTML or XHTML format and may provide navigation to other web pages via hypertext links. Web pages are requested and served from web servers using HyperText Transfer Protocol (HTTP). Web pages may be static or dynamic. Scripting web pages on client and server side make web pages more responsive to user input.
Website: A website is a collection of web pages. A website is a set of related web pages served from a single web domain. All publicly accessible websites collectively constitute the World Wide Web. The pages of a website can be accessed using its Uniform Resource Locator (URL) called web address.
Web browser: The first web browser was invented in 1990 by Sir Tim Berners Lee. It was called WorldWideWeb (No spaces) and was later renamed Nexus. A web browser is an application software for retrieving, pre-setting and traversing information resources on the World Wide Web.
HTTP (HyperText Transfer Protocol): This term was first introduced by Ted Nelson. The HyperText Transfer Protocol is an application protocol for distributed, collaborative hypermedia information systems.
HTML is the mother tongue of your browser. HTML was invented by a scientist called Tim Berners Lee in 1990. The purpose was to make it easier for scientists at different universities to gain access to each other’s research documents. The project became so popular that it led to the foundation for the web as we know it today.
HTML is not a programming language, it is a markup language used to present information on the internet. To see the HTML code of a page on the internet, simply click view in the menu bar of your browser and choose source.
In the development of web sites, HTML plays an important role. HTML is a HyperText Markup Language that is interpreted by the web browser and displays the content and makes this content available across the computer network. The information of the website is accessible by anybody across the globe at any time. An HTML contains the instructions to the web browser in the form of tags. Tags are the instructions written in angular (<>) brackets. Tag begins with open angular bracket i.e., less than (<) symbol and close angular bracket i.e., greater than (>) symbol. Tags are of two types, one is opening tags and another is closing tags. Opening tags are written inside the angular brackets and closing tags are also written inside the angular brackets but preceded with the forward (/) slash. Let's have a look at the general structure of an HTML.
<HTML>
<TITLE>
<HEAD></HEAD>
</TITLE>
<BODY>
</BODY>
</HTML>
<HTML> is a tag used to indicate it is the beginning of the HTML program. <TITLE> is a tag used to display the text at the title bar of the web browser window. Under the <HEAD> tag we generally used to write the text that appears in the title bar of the web browser. <BODY> tag is used to display the content of the user onto the canvas of the browser window. Content could be text, graphics, animation, audio or video. You can observe that each opening tag is closed. </HTML> is a closing tag for the opening tag <HTML>, </TITLE> tag is a closing tag for the opening tag <TITLE>, </HEAD> tag is a closing tag for the opening tag <HEAD> similarly, we have </BODY> tag is a closing tag for opening tag <BODY>.
Note: Some tags do not have a closing tag. All tags in HTML are case insensitive, meaning that the tags can be written in either lowercase letters or uppercase letters. But it is good programming practice to write the tags in capital letters to distinguish them from the normal text.
Basic Requirements:
-          The user must have the basic knowledge of using a web browser.
-          Computer with editors such as notepad, sublime, notepad++ or vscode if your computer is having windows operating system. The editor vscode offers code writing shortcuts and helpful features. Editors like textedit, sublime text or Komposer can be used with the Mac operating system.
-          Web browsers such as firefox mozilla or Internet Explorer 9 and above or google chrome. Here in this book we use notepad for writing HTML tags.
HTML file extension: we save an HTML file with an extension either .htm or .html. It is a normal practice of writing three letter extensions and it is perfectly fine even if we use .html.
Open the text editor – notepad shown in the following steps:
Step 1: Click on the windows button and type notepad.
Step 2: click on the notepad icon to open the notepad text editor as shown below.
Step 3: write an HTML code in the notepad and save it with .htm or .html extension.
Step 4: Click on file menu and click on save option and choose the location where you want to save your file. I have created a folder named HTML programs in F drive and saved with the name SAMPLE.HTML.
Step 5: open in the browser by double clicking on the saved file or open the browser click on file menu and click on open option and select the saved file. Click on open. It shows the output as shown below.
Commenting in an HTML: there are two types of comments – single line and multiline.
Single line comment represented as, <!Comment Text Here>
Exclamation (!) mark is used inside the angular braces.
Multiline comment represented as, <!-- multiline
​        comment-->

CHAPTER 2
BASIC TAGS
Introduction: HTML tags are the keywords that are recognized by the web browsers and interpret their meaning and display the appropriate information on the web page. Each head tag represents an object and objects have properties; these properties are called attributes. Let us learn some of the basic tags of HTML with their properties.
Headline tags: the headline tags are used to display headings on the page in various sizes. These tags can be used for providing main and sub-headings to the web page. The HTML document supports 6 levels of heading tags <H1> to <H6>. The size of the text within the <H1> tag is the largest and <H6> is the smallest. Each of these headings always begins on a newline.
Example:
<! Demonstration of Heading tags>
<html>
<body>
<h1>Heading 1</h1>
<h2>Heading 2</h2>
<h3>Heading 3</h3>
<h4>Heading 4</h4>
<h5>Heading 5</h5>
<h6>Heading 6</h6>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Attributes for heading tag: headings are displayed in the left-hand side of the browser by default. In order to make to be center or right aligned we use the attribute called align and its values are
Left
Center
Right
<!Example to use center align attribute of heading tag.>
<html>
<body>
<h1  align="center">Heading 1</h1>
<h2>Heading 2</h2>
<h3>Heading 3</h3>
<h4>Heading 4</h4>
<h5>Heading 5</h5>
</body>
</html>
Output:
<!Example to use right align attribute of heading tag.>
<html>
<body>
<h1  align="right">Heading 1</h1>
<h2>Heading 2</h2>
<h3>Heading 3</h3>
<h4>Heading 4</h4>
<h5>Heading 5</h5>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Paragraph: we can organise the text in a proper alignment using paragraphs. To perform this operation in HTML we use <p>…</p> tags. In the text document the paragraphs appear with blank lines on the top and in the bottom of the paragraph.
Example:
Attributes for paragraph tag: paragraphs are displayed in the left-hand side of the browser by default. In order to make to be center or right aligned we use the attribute called align and its values are
Left
Center
Right
<!Example to use center attribute of paragraph tag.>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Paragraph Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<P align=center>This is the first paragraph</p>
<P>This is the second paragraph</p>
<P>This is the third paragraph</p>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
<!Example to use right attribute of paragraph tag.>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Paragraph Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<P align=right>This is the first paragraph</p>
<P>This is the second paragraph</p>
<P>This is the third paragraph</p>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Line break tag: if you wish to bring the cursor down to the next line and provide a break in between the text then you can use <br> tag and any text following it will start in a newline. As I told you that some tags do not need closing tags and such tags are called empty elements. They are called so because no content goes in between them.
<!A line of text without the use of break tag>
<!A line of text without the use of break tag>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Without break tag</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
Hello there! My name is Hanumanth Ladwa. Good Morning
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Now the same code can be written with the break tag as,
<!A line of text without the use of break tag>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>With break tag</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
Hello there!<br>My name is Hanumanth Ladwa.<br>Good Morning
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Centered content: Some times while organizing the text we require to align it to the center of the table or center of the page. Once we come across tables we will discuss how to center the text in the cell in the forthcoming section.
Example:
<!Example to demonstrate the centered content>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Centered Content Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
The text is normally on to the left hand side
<center>This line of text is centered using center tag</center>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Horizontal rule: you can make sections in the canvas area of the browser window by using a tag called <hr> to make separate sections in the window.
Example:
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Horizontal Rule Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
This text appears before the horizontal rule.
<hr>
This text appears after the horizontal rule.
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Note that this tag is also an empty element because it doesn’t need opening and closing tags.
If you wish to change the color of the horizontal rule, you can do that by using color attribute as,
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Horizontal Rule Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
This text appears before the horizontal rule.
<hr color=red>
This text appears after the horizontal rule.
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
We can use the attribute size and width of <HR> element as shown below.
Size: the thickness of the line is expressed in terms of pixels.
<HR SIZE=”Value”>
The possible values are, 1, 2, 3, and so on.
Width: the width of the line can be specified in terms of percentage of the window width.
<HR WIDTH=”Value”>
The possible values for the width can be specified in terms of percentage size of the window. For example, 25%, 50%, 75% and so on.
<!HTML code to demonstrate width and size attributes of HR element>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Horizontal Rule Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<p>This text appears before the horizontal rule.
<hr color=red width=200 size=15>
This text appears after the horizontal rule.
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Bold and Italic: To make words eye catchy we use bold and italic. Bold makes the text under the <B> and </B> tag thicker and Italic makes the text under the <I> and </I> tag tilted a little on to the right hand side.
Example:
<!HTML code to make text Bold>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Bold Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<p>This is how we make a specific word <b>bold</b><p>
<p>In some situations it is <b>important</b> to remember <b>help</b>received</p>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
<!HTML code to make text Italic>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Italic Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<p>Where there is a <I>will</I> there is a <I>way.</I><p>
<p>Help the poor, <I>God</I> will help you.</p>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
You can also combine both bold and italicized text together as,
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Bold and Italic Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<p>An <B><I>ounce of action</I></B> is worth a <B><I>ton of theory.</I></B></p>
<p align=right>by Friedrich Engles</p>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Superscript and subscript: superscript is used to display the text under the tag <sup> little above the line of normal text. This is normally used in Mathematics to write squares and cubes of numbers.
<!HTML code to demonstrate superscript>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Superscript Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<p>z=x<sup>2</sup>+y<sup>2</sup>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Subscript is used to display the text under the tag <sub> a little below the normal text. This is normally used in Chemistry to write Chemical equations.
<!HTML code to demonstrate subscript>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Subscript Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<p>The life giving gas is O<sub>2</sub></p>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Strong and emphasis: The tag <strong> is used to indicate that the content under it has strong importance. By default, browsers will show the contents of a <strong> tag in bold as below.
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Strong Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<p><strong>Success</strong> comes before work only in <strong>dictionary.</strong><p>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Similarly, the tag <em> is used to emphasize the text under it. Usually this tag displays the text in Italic form as below.
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Emphasis Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<p><em>success</em> follow <em>worthy.</em><p>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Quotations: The tag <q> is used to put quotation marks to the text under this tag as shown below.
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Quotation Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<p><q>Mother is the first Teacher</q></p>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Abbreviations and acronyms: these are the short forms read as a word are called acronyms and abbreviations.
Example:
<!HTML code to demonstrate abbreviation and acronym>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Abbreviation and Acronym Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<p>My name is <abbr title="Professor">Prof. </abbr>Hanumanth Ladwa I am a professor in a college.
<p>My students are interested in the operations of <acronym title="Indian Space Research Organization">ISRO</acronym><p>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
When you hover a mouse over Prof. and ISRO they show the long form of these abbreviations and acronyms.
Making changes to contents: the text inserted and deleted can be seen by using <ins> and <del> tags respectively.
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Insert and delete Content</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<p>Bring the <del>worst</del> <ins>best</ins> in you!<p>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
The tag <s> is used to indicate something that is no longer accurate. The content under the <s> tag is displayed with a line through the center. Let's take an example,
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Sales Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<P>T-Shirt:</P>
<P><s>Was $10</S></P>
<P>Now only at <B>$5</B></P>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Larger text: The text placed inside the element <big>…</big > will be displayed as a one size larger than the rest of the content surrounding it.
<!HTML code to illustrate larger text>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Larger Text Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<p>This is a <big>Larger</big> text.
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Smaller text: The text placed inside the element <small>…</small > will be displayed as a one size smaller than the rest of the content surrounding it.
<!HTML code to illustrate smaller text>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Smaller Text Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<p>This is a <small>Smaller</small> text.
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Strike text: The text can be strike out with the help of a tag called <strike>…</strike>.
Example:
<!HTML code to illustrate strike through>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Strike Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<p>This is a code to demonstrate <strike>strikethrough</strike>.<p>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Scrolling text: Text can be scrolled within the browser window using a tag called <MARQUEE>…</MARQUEE> as shown below.
<!HTML code to demonstrate MARQUEE>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Marquee Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<MARQUEE>This is a scrolling marquee text</MARQUEE>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
By default marquee scrolls the text from right to left you can change its direction of movement from left to right, top to bottom and bottom to top. Let us see all these directions of marquee using an attribute called direction. Its values are left, right.
<!HTML code to demonstrate left to right marquee>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Marquee Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<MARQUEE direction=RIGHT>This is a Marquee text moving from left to write</MARQUEE>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Font setting tag: the element <FONT> can be used to change the appearance, that is, it is used to style the text. The attributes of this tag are, FACE, SIZE, COLOR.
FACE attribute is used to set the font design.
Syntax:
<FONT FACE=”Value”>
Text here
</FONT>
Some of the values of a FACE attribute are given below.
Times new roman
Arial black
Arial
Courier,
Bookman old style etc.
You can refer to other font faces from MS Word fonts. Otherwise, you can download and use any font style.
SIZE attribute is used to increase or decrease the size of the text.
Syntax:
<FONT SIZE=”Value”>
Text here
</FONT>
The possible values are 1, 2, 3, and so on.
The COLOR attribute is used to paint the text with different colors.
Syntax:
<FONT COLOR=”Value”>
Text here
</FONT>
The possible value for the COLOR attribute can be specified in two ways, one is using the name of the color or using hexadecimal color constants.
Color
Red
Green
Blue
White
Black
Gray
Dark red
Dark green
Yellow
So on
<!HTML code to illustrate font attributes>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Font Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<FONT FACE="ARIAL" SIZE=15 COLOR=BLUE>This the formatted text</FONT>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Computer output tags: the various types of output will be displayed as shown below.
<html>
<HEAD><TITLE>Computer Output Demo</TITLE></HEAD>
<body>
<code>Computer code</code>
<br />
<kbd>Keyboard input</kbd>
<br />
<tt>Teletype text</tt>
<br />
<samp>Sample text</samp>
<br />
<var>Computer variable</var>
<br />
<p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Computer output tags: Give a try of the following computer output tags yourself as shown in the program above.
TAG
DESCRIPTION
<code>
It defines computer code text.
<kbd>
It defines keyboard text.
<samp>
It defines sample computer code.
<tt>
It defines teletype text.
<var>
It defines a variable.
<pre>
It defines a preformatted text.
<listing>
It is deprecated. Use <pre> instead.
<plaintext>
It is deprecated. Use <pre> instead.
<xmp>
It is deprecated. Use <pre> instead.
Address: The address can be written inside the <ADDRESS>…</ADDRESS> tag.
Example:
<!HTML code to demonstrate address tag>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Address Tag Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<ADDRESS>Hanumanth Ladwa<br>Hubballi<br>Karnataka<br>India.</ADDRESS>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Text Direction: if your computer browser supports bidirectional override (BDO), then the line of text will be written from left to right (rtl) as shown in the following example.
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Text Direction Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<bdo dir="ltr">Original text: This is the right to left text</bdo><br>
Reversed Text: <bdo dir="rtl">This is the right to left text</bdo>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:

CHAPTER 3
HTML LISTS
Introduction: we know how to organize the items in a list. When we are asked to make a list of shopping or items required for food, we write then one below the other with the hyphen (-) or numbers or any other symbol. The same type of list can be generated in HTML.
Lists are classified as,
-          Unordered lists
-          Ordered lists
-          Definition lists
-          Nested lists
Unordered lists: An unordered list can be incorporated in the web page using <UL> tag. Under this <UL> tag we write list items using <LI> tag. The <UL> tag uses the attribute TYPE to represent
-          Circle
-          Disc
-          Square
By default, it displays disc as shown in the below example.
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Unordered List Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<UL>
<LI>Orange</LI>
<LI>Banana</LI>
<LI>Mango</LI>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
<!HTML code to demonstrate unordered disc list>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Unordered Disc List Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<ul type="disc">
<li>HTML</li>
<li>CSS</li>
<li>JavaScript</li>
</ul>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
<!HTML code to demonstrate unordered circle list>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Unordered Circle List Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<ul type="circle">
<li>HTML</li>
<li>CSS</li>
<li>JavaScript</li>
</ul>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
<!HTML code to demonstrate unordered square list>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Unordered Square List Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<ul type="square">
<li>HTML</li>
<li>CSS</li>
<li>JavaScript</li>
</ul>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Ordered lists: An ordered list can be incorporated in the web page using <OL> tag. Under this <OL> tag we write list items using <LI> tag. The <OL> tag uses the attribute TYPE to represent the sequence of numbers like,
-          Plain numbers
-          Small Roman numbers
-          Capital Roman numbers
-          Small letters
-          Capital letters
By default, it displays plain numbers as shown in the below example.
<!HTML code to demonstrate plain numbers>

<HEAD><TITLE>Ordered Plain Numbers List Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<OL>
<LI>C Programming</LI>
<LI>C++ Programming</LI>
<LI>Java Programming</LI>
</OL>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:

<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Ordered Small Roman Numbers List Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<OL TYPE=i>
<LI>C Programming</LI>
<LI>C++ Programming</LI>
<LI>Java Programming</LI>
</OL>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:

<LI>C Programming</LI>
<LI>C++ Programming</LI>
<LI>Java Programming</LI>
</OL>
</BODY>
</HTML>

</OL>

</HTML>

Definition lists: It is a list of items together with a description of each item. A definition list starts with a <DL> tag. Each item starts with a <DT> tag. Each description starts with a <DD> tag. Let us take an example,

CHAPTER 4
LINKS
Introduction: Links are the text or image by clicking on them to take to the other web page or within the same page. Links are used to navigate from one place to another.
Hyperlink and anchors: An anchor is a web page that enables objects to be used as hyperlinks. A hyperlink in a page is an area that opens a new site or page. The objects used to create hyperlinks can be either text or image. Anchors and hyperlinks can be defined by the tag <A>…</A>.
Hyperlink: The hyperlinked text can be easily identified as it will be of a different color against the other text in the document and will be underlined. When the mouse moves, the cursor changes its shape. When the mouse pointer is moved over any text and if it turns to a pointing hand, it means that a link has been established. Clicking on the link will either:
-          Open a new section within the same web page which is called a page jump.
-          Create a link to another page within your own website.
-          Create a link to a web page from a different website.
When designing an HTML document, creating hyperlinks is the last step. This is because when a complete document is created, reference to other parts of the same document can be given.
Anchor: anchor is a container element used to create a hyperlink. The object to which the link has to be made is defined by the hypertext reference or HREF attribute. Identifying an anchor on a page is fairly easy as the text anchors are underlined and are in a different color from the regular text.
Internal and external links: internal links are those which have reference to the web pages within the same website.
External links are those which have reference to the webpages on the other websites.
When links are created it is essential to,
-          Specify the URL of the file to which the link is created.
-          Specify the text or graphics on which the user has to click.
The tag <A> is used to create a hyperlink that takes the user to the specified target location in a document.
Syntax:
<A HREF=”URL”>Text to click here</A>
<A NAME=Value>…</A>
Here, HREF stands for Hypertext Reference that contains the URL.
NAME attribute is used to identify a location within the same HTML document.
<!HTML code to demonstrate an internal link>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Internal Link Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<A HREF="F:\HTML programs\Bold.html">Open Bold Webpage</A>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Once we click on the Open Bold Webpage link, it takes us to the Bold.html file which is saved in the F:\HTML program folder in my computer. It takes us to the following window.
<!HTML code to demonstrate an external link>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>External Link Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<A HREF="https:\\www.gmail.com">Open gmail.com</A>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Once we click on the Open gmail.com link, it takes us to https://www.gmail.com webpage.
Creating links within the same document: Till now we have studied how to navigate from one webpage to another webpage using internal and external links. Now we will create a link within the same document. Bookmark the location with the NAME attribute of the <A> tag and then create a link with the HREF attribute of <A>.
Syntax:
<A NAME=”Value”>
Here, Value can be any valid string of characters.
Syntax to create a link,
<A HREF=”#Value”>
Note: The value in the hyperlink must be same as the value in the name attribute of the anchor tag and must be preceded by the # sign.
When clicked on the link, the named section of the page will be displayed on top.
Example:
<!HTML code to demonstrate Link within the Document>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Link Within the Document Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<A HREF=#c1>Chapter 1</A>
<A HREF=#c2>Chapter 2</A>
<A HREF=#c3>Chapter 3</A>
<p><A NAME=c1>Chapter 1</A></p>
<p>This chapter 1 contains the important concepts<p>
<p><A NAME=c2>Chapter 2</A></p>
<p>This chapter 2 contains the important concepts<p>
<p><A NAME=c3>Chapter 3</A></p>
<p>This chapter 3 contains the important concepts<p>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
If you click on Chapter 1 hyperlink, it scrolls down to Chapter 1 as shown below.
If you click on Chapter 2 hyperlink, it scrolls down to Chapter 2 as shown below.
If you click on Chapter 3 hyperlink, it scrolls down to Chapter 3 as shown below.
In the HTML code above, an anchor by name c1, c2 and c3 is defined, followed by a paragraph. This is the target location that is to be displayed when the Chapter 1, Chapter 2 and Chapter 3 links are clicked.
Now it is required to create a link to this anchor, so that when the user clicks the hyperlink the user can directly jump to this paragraph.
Bookmark Link: Bookmark link is used when the content in the webpage is too long and we have scrolled down in the window and we want to scroll to the top of the content in such situation we use #top with <A> tag as shown in the following example.
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Bookmark Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<p> We write paragraph 1</p>
<p> We write paragraph 2</p>
<p> We write paragraph 3</p>
<p> We write paragraph 4</p>
<p> We write paragraph 5</p>
<p> We write paragraph 6</p>
<p> We write paragraph 7</p>
<p> We write paragraph 8</p>
<p> We write paragraph 9</p>
<p> We write paragraph 10</p>
<p> We write paragraph 11</p>
<p> We write paragraph 12</p>
<A HREF=#top>Back to Top</A>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
When we scroll down, we can see the link at the bottom of the window as shown below.
Now click on the Back to Top link to scroll to top of the window as shown below.
Open a link in a new browser window: To open a link in a new browser window we use the target attribute of <A> tag. We open a link in a new window because we don’t want the visitor to leave our website.
<!HTML code to illustrate opening a link in a new window>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Open in New window Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<A HREF=Headings.html target=_blank>Open in new page</A>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
When we click on Open in the new page it navigates to the Headings.html file which is saved in the F:\HTML programs folder of my computer as shown below.
Now you can observe that the link is opening a new window in the same web browser without closing the previous window.
Creating link to an email address: Normally websites will have an email hyperlink at the bottom of the web page to the site owner so that the users can send a feedback regarding the website. On clicking this link the email editor will be automatically launched where the user can straight away type his message. To do this, MAILTO: attribute of <A> tag is used.
Syntax:
<A HREF=MAILTO:Value>Email Me</A>
<!HTML code to demonstrate creating email link>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Email Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<p>Please mail me for further improvement of this book at</p>
<A HREF=MAILTO:hanumant50.ladwa@gmail.com?subject=Feedback>Mail Me!</A>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
When we click on the Mail Me! link it takes you to the following window to send your feedback.

CHAPTER 5
IMAGES, AUDIO AND VIDEO
Introduction: images can be helpful to beautify and to explain the complex concepts of your webpage in a simple way. The tag used to inert an image is called <IMG>.
Syntax:
<IMG SRC=”Image URL” … attribute-list/>
The image tag is an empty tag because there is no closing tag for it.
Example: <!HTML code to insert an image>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Inserting an Image Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<IMG SRC="F:\HTML programs\Hanumanth Ladwa.png" height=300 width=300 alt=”Hanumanth Ladwa”/>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
ALT attribute is providing a text description of the image which describes the images if you cannot see it.
Note: If the browser doesn’t display the image, the ALT text appears.
Change the browser and get the output as shown below.
HTML supports image formats such as JPEG, PNG and GIF. In the above code, the attribute SRC is a source of image and the attributes height and width are used to resize the image.
Note:
-          Image names are case sensitive.
-          If one browser doesn’t support the image then change the browser, open the file with another browser.
Set image border: By default, every image has a border. The only thing is we need to specify the border size. To do this we need to use the BORDER property of the <IMG> tag. For the above HTML code, we add border property see the result as shown below.
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Inserting an Image with Border Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<IMG SRC="F:\HTML programs\Hanumanth Ladwa.png" height=300 width=300 border=2/>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Set image alignment: Images in HTML by default left aligned. You can specify RIGHT or CENTER value to the attribute ALIGN OF <IMG> tag.
<!HTML code to align image on to right>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Inserting an Image align on right Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<IMG SRC="F:\HTML programs\Hanumanth Ladwa.png" height=300 width=300 border=2 align="right"/>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Aligning an image onto the center of the browser window is left to you as Homework.

<!HTML code to demonstrate background image>

<BODY BACKGROUND="F:\HTML programs\background image.jpeg">

<H1>This is a header 1 text.</H1>
<H1>This is a header 1 text.</H1>
<H1>This is a header 1 text.</H1>
<H1>This is a header 1 text.</H1>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Note: if the image you are using is smaller than the screen, the image will be replicated until it fills the entire screen.

<H1>This is a header 1 text.</H1>
<H1>This is a header 1 text.</H1>
<H1>This is a header 1 text.</H1>
<H1>This is a header 1 text.</H1>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Note: if you do not get the output, change the browser as I have changed and executed the above program using Internet Explorer.

text.

/>

this text.
</p>

Linking images in HTML: We can make images clickable. When the user clicks on the image, it should navigate to another webpage. To do this we use <A> tag, inside the anchor tag we use <IMG> tag. Let’s have a look at an example.

Playing audio files: to play audio we need to use two tags,
-          <AUDIO> tag
-          <SOURCE> ​tag with a path to your file and the type of audio file.
Syntax:

<SOURCE SRC=”…” TYPE=”…”>
</SOURCE>
</AUDIO>
<!HTML code to demonstrate playing audio file>

</SOURCE>
</AUDIO>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:

</SOURCE>
</AUDIO>
</BODY>
</HTML>

</SOURCE>
</AUDIO>
</BODY>
</HTML>

<!HTML code to demonstrate YouTube video>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Playing Video from YouTube</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<iframe width="560" height="315" src="https://www.youtube.com/embed/rgVRycSaL8g" title="YouTube video player" frameborder="0" allow="accelerometer; autoplay; clipboard-write; encrypted-media; gyroscope; picture-in-picture" allowfullscreen></iframe>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Playing YouTube video automatically: To play YouTube video automatically, use autoplay=1 in the <iframe> tag as shown in the following example.
<!HTML code to demonstrate playing YouTube video automatically>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Automatic Playing Video from YouTube</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<iframe width="560" height="315" src="https://www.youtube.com/embed/rgVRycSaL8g?autoplay=1" title="YouTube video player" frameborder="0" allow="accelerometer; autoplay; clipboard-write; encrypted-media; gyroscope; picture-in-picture;" allowfullscreen></iframe>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Similarly, you can also use MUTE=1 attribute to mute the video. Give a try to this HTML code.
YouTube Controls: You can make YouTube video controls to disable by using controls=0. Default value of controls is 1. Let’s have a look at an example.
<!HTML code to demonstrate Disable YouTube Video Control>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Disable YouTube Video Controls</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<iframe width="560" height="315" src="https://www.youtube.com/embed/rgVRycSaL8g?controls=0" title="YouTube video player" frameborder="0" allow="accelerometer; autoplay; clipboard-write; encrypted-media; gyroscope; picture-in-picture;" allowfullscreen></iframe>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:

CHAPTER 6
TABLES
Introduction: tabular representation is the best way to present grouped information. Tables are used for arranging the layout of a web page. The contents on the page look organized with the use of tables, which adds to its visual appeal.
Tables are defined using the <TABLE> tag. A table is formed by a collection of rows and columns. Each row is created using <TR> tag and each data in a row is created using <td> tag. The letters TR stands for Table row and TD stands for Table Data, which is the content of a data cell. A data cell can contain text, images, lists, paragraphs, forms, tables and so on.
The HTML tag for creating a table are:
<TABLE>…</TABLE>
Syntax:
<TABLE ALIGN=”…” BORDER=”…” CELL PADDING=”…” CELL SPACING=”…” WIDTH=”…”>…</TABLE>
The table tag can have the following attributes:
ALIGN – it specifies the alignment of the table in the page. Values of align attribute are LEFT, RIGHT AND CENTER.
BORDER – it specifies the width of the border to be drawn around all table cells, by default there is no border drawn. Values possible are 1,2,3, … so on.
CELL PADDING – it specifies the space between the content inside the edge of a cell. Values possible are 1,2,3,… so on.
CELL SPACING – it specifies the amount of space between cells in a table. Values possible are 1,2,3,… so on.
WIDTH – it is used to specify the width of the table. The maximum width can be the maximum size of the monitor in pixels.
<TR>…</TR>
This stands for table row. To add a row in a table, <TR> tag is used. For each row there will be a set of table row tags. The properties of each row are defined within the row tags.
Syntax:
<TABLE>
<TR BGCOLOR=”…”>
…
</TR>
</TABLE>
The attribute BGCOLOR is used to set the background color of the row.
<TD>…</TD>
This stands for table data. The table data tags define each cell in the table. They must be nested within the table row tags.
Syntax:
<TABLE>
<TR>
<TD ALIGN=”…” COLSPAN=”…” ROWSPAN=”…”></TD>
</TR>
</TABLE>
Here, the ALIGN attribute sets the position of the cell content in one of the three positions LEFT, CENTER AND RIGHT.
The COLSPAN attribute is used to merge more than one column together to form a single column.
The ROWSPAN attribute is used to merge more than one row together to form a single row.
It is not necessary for all the rows to have the same number of columns; the rows with less number of columns will be padded with blank cells. A cell definition can contain all the HTML tags that are normally used in the body of an HTML document.
Any of the above-mentioned table tags if placed outside the <TABLE>…</TABLE> tag will be ignored by the browsers.
Table captions and headers: normally every table displayed in the page will have a little that describes the contents of the table. Also, each column of the table has a heading which names the information stored in it.
The tags used are:
<CAPTION>…</CAPTION>
This is used for the Title/Caption for a table. <CAPTION> elements should appear inside the <TABLE> tag but not inside table rows or cells. Captions are always horizontally centered with respect to the table, and they may have their lines wrapped to fit within the width of the table.
Attribute for the tag is:
ALIGN: it specifies the location of the caption. Possible values are TOP AND BOTTOM.
<TH>…<TH>
These tags are used to designate a cell to be a header cell and are to be used within the <TR>…<TR> tags.
By default, the table border is 0. It doesn’t show any borders at all. In most of the situations, a border is required to display. Border is an outline for the table. Let’s see how the table looks without the border.
<!HTML code to demonstrate table without border>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Table Without Border Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<table>
<tr>
<td>ROW ONE COLUMN ONE</td>
<td>ROW ONE COLUMN TWO</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>ROW TWO COLUMN ONE</td>
<td>ROW TWO COLUMN TWO</td>
</tr>
</table>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
<!HTML code to demonstrate table with a border>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Table Demo</TITLE><HEAD>

</tr>
<tr>
<td>ROW TWO COLUMN ONE</td>
<td>ROW TWO COLUMN TWO</td>
</tr>
</table>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:

CHAPTER 7
FORMS
Introduction: We often use forms to collect information from a visitor to your site. Here we talk about online forms. On paper, forms are made up of areas where users need to enter text, boxes to check, options to choose from and multiple selection of items in the lists and so on. In a similar way, online forms consist of text boxes, checkboxes, radio buttons, dropdown lists, buttons and so on. Using all of these components, we can create a form to collect all kinds of information from visitors to your site.
You might have seen online job application forms, college admission forms, feedback forms, complaint forms, google search engines and so on. that consists of various fields to receive the information from the user.
Online job application form for hidden field. In the below application form, there is a dropdown list for disability, if we select yes, then automatically the hidden field appears as shown in the second image.
To create a form in HTML we use a <FORM>…<FORM> tag. All the components such as, text boxes, checkboxes, radio buttons, list boxes and so on. are enclosed within the form tags.
Note: The data filled in the form will neither be stored on your local computer nor on the server. This chapter teaches you how to design an online form. Storing data to the server is out of scope of this book.
Forms can be classified in to three sections namely:
-          Form header
-          Input fields
-          Action buttons
Syntax:
<HTML>
<BODY>
​<FORM>
​FORM ELEMENTS
​</FORM>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Input elements: input elements are form elements that can accept input from the user. The following are some of the input elements that can be added to a form.
-          Text field
-          Password field
-          Hidden field
-          Text area
-          Checkbox
-          Radio button
-          Dropdown menu
-          Submit button
-          Reset button
-          Image button
<INPUT> is the tag used to create any of the form elements and the TYPE attribute determines the specific sort of form element to be created.
Syntax:
<INPUT TYPE=”” NAME=”” VALUE=”” ALIGN=”” SIZE=”” MAXLENGTH=””>
Here, TYPE attribute specifies the type of the form element will be added to the form.
NAME specifies every element in the form must have a name to identify it. It appears as the label in the form and should be unique.
VALUE specifies a default value to the control.
ALIGN it is used for aligning controls with respect to form margins with one of the values, left, center or right.
SIZE specifies the horizontal size of the textbox.
MAXLENGTH specifies the maximum number of characters that the user can enter.
Adding a textbox: A textbox allows a user to enter data such as characters and numbers. In order to add a textbox, you need to do the following:
<INPUT TYPE=”TEXT” NAME=”sname” SIZE=””30 MAXLENGTH=”30” VALUE=””>
Here, TEXT indicates that textbox is the interface element.
NAME=”sname” is the name of the textbox by which it will be identified.
SIZE=”30” specifies the horizontal size of the text box. By default, the size of the textbox is 20.
MAXLENGTH=”30” it is optional, and is used to specify the maximum number of characters that the user can enter into the textbox.
VALUE=”” specify any default text in the textbox. In the above case since we have not specified anything within the quotes the textbox will appear blank.
<!HTML code to demonstrate text field>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Text Field Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<FORM>
First Name:<INPUT TYPE="TEXT" NAME="FirstName" VALUE="Suraj"><br><br>
Last Name:<INPUT TYPE="TEXT" NAME="LastName" VALUE="Naik"><br>
</FORM>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Adding a password field: this is the same as a text box, except that whatever the user enters in it is masked with an asterisk or some similar character so that no one can see what the user typed.
<INPUT TYPE=”PASSWORD” NAME=”Password” SIZE=”10”>
Even though the display on the screen is masked with special characters such as asterisk, the contents are transmitted in plain form.
TYPE=”PASSWORD” the value PASSWORD indicates that PASSWORD is the interface element.
NAME=”Password” is the name of the password field.
SIZE=”10” is the maximum size of the password field. It is the same as that for a textbox.
<!HTML code to demonstrate password field>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Password Field Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<FORM>
User Name:<INPUT TYPE="TEXT" NAME="UserName"><br><br>
Password:<INPUT TYPE="PASSWORD" NAME="psw"><br>
</FORM>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Hidden field: In some of the online job applications, you might have seen the option for applicant’s disability, once the applicant checks this option, the hidden textbox appears to receive a percentage of disability. In some other cases, if the online application form consists of an option button for additional qualification, if the applicant has an additional qualification to be entered, clicking on this option opens up a new textbox to receive the data from the user. This is how the hidden field is used.
<!HTML code to illustrate hidden field>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Hidden Field Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<FORM>
First Name:<INPUT TYPE="TEXT" NAME="fName"><br><br>
<INPUT TYPE="HIDDEN" NAME="country" VALUE="India">
</FORM>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Note that the hidden field is not visible, once some event occurs then the hidden field appears as discussed. As there is no event occurring here in this case hence it is permanently hidden.
Text area: in certain cases, it might be necessary to accept several lines of text from the user, in such a case use the <TEXTAREA> tag.
Syntax:
<TEXTAREA ROWS=”” COLS=”” WRAP=””>
Like a textbox there are similar attributes to control the size of the data entry area, assign a default value and the name to the control. Other than these, multiple line text input has additional attributes:
ROWS define the height of the text area.
COLS define the width of the text area.
WRAP defines the wrapping of the text in the text area. The possible values are:
OFF no wrapping takes place and the text will be sent as it is typed.
VIRTUAL word wraps take place, but the entire text is still sent as a long line.
PHYSICAL word wraps take place and the text is sent at all wrap points.
<!HTML code to demonstrate textarea>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Textarea Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<FORM>
<TEXTAREA ROWS="4" COLS="20">
My name is Hanumanth Ladwa. Completed a Masters degree in Computer Science and taught thousands of students both online and offline. I have more than a decade of teaching experience in various subjects.
</TEXTAREA>
</FORM>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Checkbox: A checkbox is like a toggle switch where users can select or deselect the item. If the checkbox is selected then a value TRUE is returned with the checkbox name else FALSE is returned.
<INPUT TYPE=”CHECKBOX” NAME=”” VALUE=”” CHECKED>
TYPE=CHECKBOX the value CHECKBOX indicates that CHECKBOX is the interface element.
NAME is the name by which it will be identified.
VALUE will be assigned to the checkbox when it is in the checked state.
CHECKED this is optional and it causes the checkbox to be ON, that is checked, when the form is first created.
<!HTML code to demonstrate checkbox>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Checkbox Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<FORM>
<INPUT TYPE="CHECKBOX" NAME="VEHICLE" VALUE="Bike">I have a Bike<br>
<INPUT TYPE="CHECKBOX" NAME="VEHICLE" VALUE="Car" CHECKED>I have a Car<br>
<INPUT TYPE="CHECKBOX" NAME="VEHICLE" VALUE="Bicycle">I have a Bicycle<br>
</FORM>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Radio Button: radio buttons are used when the user has to make a selection among multiple choices and is always used in a group. For example while accepting the gender of a user, you need to specify two radio buttons “Male” and “Female”, which belong to the same group called gender so that a user will be able to select either Male or Female but not both. The attributes for this are the same as that of the checkbox.
<INPUT TYPE=”RADIO” NAME=”gender” VALUE=”Male” CHECKED>Male
<INPUT TYPE=”RADIO” NAME=”gender” VALUE=”Female”>Female
TYPE= RADIO indicates that RADIO is the interface element.
NAME=”gender” is the name of the radio group
VALUE=”Male” and VALUE=”Female” one radio button has a value Male and the other has a value “Female”. Based upon which radio button was selected only one of these values will be assigned to the radio group gender.
CHECKED, the radio button with this attribute selected will be checked by default.
<!HTML code to demonstrate radio button>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Radio Button Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<FORM>
<INPUT TYPE="RADIO" NAME="GENDER" VALUE="MALE" CHECKED>MALE<BR>
<INPUT TYPE="RADIO" NAME="GENDER" VALUE="FEMALE">FEMALE<BR>
</FORM>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Dropdown menu: A selection list displays a list of options from which the user can select an item. The select tag is used to define the selection list in a form and should be defined within the form tag. The option tag is attached to the select tag to display a list of options.
<SELECT NAME=”Listname”>
<OPTION>Option1
<OPTION>Option2
</SELECT>
Listname is the name of the selection list and Option1 and Option2 as two options.
The user can select one of the options and the value will be assigned to the list name on selecting it. Normally, the first option will be selected by default, but you can also have an anchor option as the default. The SELECTED attribute of the <OPTION> tag is used for specifying the default selection.
Syntax:
<SELECT NAME=”Listname”>
<OPTION>Option1
<OPTION SELECTED>Option2
</SELECT>
Let us consider there is a list of countries and the option “India” is selected from the selection list, then the word India will be stored into a file, on completion of filling the form. Instead of saving the full word, it can be saved as “Ind” or with a code word while displaying the full name. This can be achieved by using the VALUE attribute of the <OPTION> tag.
<SELECT NAME=”Listname”>
<OPTION>Option1
<OPTION VALUE=””>Option2
</SELECT>
The value attribute will assign the string that is assigned to it to the name of the selected list.
<OPTION VALUE=”Ind”>India
<!HTML code to demonstrate Dropdown Menu>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Dropdown Menu Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<FORM>
<SELECT>
<OPTION VALUE="C Programming">C Programming</OPTION>
<OPTION VALUE="C++ Programming">C++ Programming</OPTION>
<OPTION VALUE="Java Programming">Java Programming</OPTION>
</SELECT>
</FORM>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Multiple selection: You can allow the user to select more than one option from this by adding multiple attributes with a value multiple. You can turn a dropdown menu into a box that shows more than one option by adding the size attribute.
<!HTML code to demonstrate multiple selection>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Multiple Selection Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<FORM>
<SELECT NAME="PROGRAMMING" SIZE="3" MULTIPLE="MULTIPLE">
<OPTION VALUE="C Programming">C Programming</OPTION>
<OPTION VALUE="C++ Programming">C++ Programming</OPTION>
<OPTION VALUE="Java Programming">Java Programming</OPTION>
</SELECT>
</FORM>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Submit Button: Once all the details of the form are entered, then this data has to be stored somewhere so that it can be reused and the form should also be cleared for another set of information to be entered. On clicking the Submit button the data in all fields will be sent to the server as a series of name/value pairs. The NAME is the name of the form element and the VALUE is whatever the user entered. From the server, the data may either be saved into a database, a text file or sent through email.
To add submit button to the form, the code is,
<INPUT TYPE=”SUBMIT” VALUE=”SUBMIT”>
TYPE=”SUBMIT” submit with default functionality is added to the form.
VALUE=”SUBMIT” This is the text that appears on the face of the button. It can be changed to anything. For example VALUE=”CLICK HERE”.
<TD ALIGN=”CENTER” COLSPAN=”2”>
Centers the submit button in the last row of the table and that spreads to both the columns.
<!HTML code to demonstrate Submit Button>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Submit Button Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<FORM>
First Name:<INPUT TYPE="TEXT" NAME="FirstName" VALUE="Suraj"><br><br>
Last Name:<INPUT TYPE="TEXT" NAME="LastName" VALUE="Naik"><br><br>
<INPUT TYPE="Submit" VALUE="Submit">
</FORM>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Reset Button: Reset button can be added to a form by specifying RESET value to the TYPE attribute of the <INPUT> tag.
<INPUT TYPE=”RESET” VALUE=”RESET”>
<!HTML code to demonstrate Reset Button>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Reset Button Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<FORM>
First Name:<INPUT TYPE="TEXT" NAME="FirstName"><br><br>
Last Name:<INPUT TYPE="TEXT" NAME="LastName"><br><br>
<INPUT TYPE="Submit" VALUE="Submit">
<BUTTON TYPE="RESET" VALUE="RESET">Reset</BUTTON>
</FORM>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Image button: The IMAGE value of the TYPE attribute is the same as the Submit button but the only difference is that the button is replaced by an image specified by the user.
<INPUT TYPE=”IMAGE” SRC=”IMAGE_NAME”>
SRC is the attribute of the INPUT tag that is used only when the value of the TYPE attribute is IMAGE. It specifies the URL where the image is located. Once the data is entered, the form should be submitted. On submitting the form the data is usually stored into a web database.
<!HTML code to demonstrate Image Button>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Image Button Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<FORM>
First Name:<INPUT TYPE="TEXT" NAME="FirstName"><br><br>
Last Name:<INPUT TYPE="TEXT" NAME="LastName"><br><br>
<INPUT TYPE="IMAGE" SRC="F:\HTML programs\MS.gif">
</FORM>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Fieldset: A fieldset is a grouping of data fields by drawing a border with a caption around data as shown in the following example.

</BODY>

Once we click on the Browse button it opens an open dialog box as shown below.
Select the file called key.txt and it looks like the following image.
When we click on upload it doesn’t perform anything. Because we have no actionable events written for it.

Date input: some time we require to input a date, this can be done by using the attribute type=”date” as shown in the following example.
