Output:
Email input: we can enter an email using the attribute type=”email” in the input tag as shown in the following example.
<!HTML code to demonstrate Email input>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Email Input Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<FORM>
<P>Input Email</P>
<INPUT TYPE="email" SIZE="50" NAME="email">
<INPUT TYPE="SUBMIT" VALUE="Submit">
</FORM>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
If the email address is not proper then it shows the error message as shown in the above diagram. If the email is accurate then it doesn’t show any error and an email will be submitted to the server.
URL input: A URL input is used when the user has been asked to enter a webpage address. This will check for validation that the user has provided as an URL.
<!HTML code to demonstrate URL input>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>URL Input Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<FORM>
<p>Kindly provide your web address:</p>
<INPUT TYPE="URL" NAME="WEBSITE" SIZE="40"/>
<INPUT TYPE="SUBMIT" VALUE="SUBMIT"/>
</FORM>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
If the URL of a website is not preceded with https then the URL input type doesn’t accept. The following window you can find the URL string will be accepted by the URL input type and once clicked on submit saves in the server.
Search input: like, how we have a single search box in google search engine in the same way we can also create a single search input type using the attribute type=”search” as shown below.
<!HTML code to demonstrate search input>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Search Input Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<FORM>
<p>Search</p>
<INPUT TYPE="SEARCH" NAME="SEARCH" SIZE="40"/>
<INPUT TYPE="SUBMIT" VALUE="SEARCH"/>
</FORM>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
One more important attribute called placeholder can be used to display text what to do in the search textbox in light gray color until the user clicks in that area as shown in the following example.
<!HTML code to demonstrate search input with a placeholder attribute>
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Search Input with Placeholder Demo</TITLE><HEAD>
<BODY>
<FORM>
<p>Search</p>
<INPUT TYPE="SEARCH" NAME="SEARCH" SIZE="40" PLACEHOLDER="Enter Keyword"/>
<INPUT TYPE="SUBMIT" VALUE="SEARCH"/>
</FORM>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:

CHAPTER 8
CASCADING STYLE SHEET (CSS)
Introduction: CSS is used to create and apply certain rules to specify how the content of an element should appear like, how the background should be, what are the styles for the text to be applied, how the elements are to be presented in the more presentable format. All in all we can change and improve the appearance of the webpage.
Advantages of CSS:
-          The same formatting style sheet can be applied to many HTML pages. Hence, it saves time.
-          It reduces the length of the code as it uses one rule for a tag that can be applied to every occurrence of the tag. Hence, less code faster loading.
-          Very easy to maintain as it requires to globally change the style that can be applied to all the web pages.
-          HTML code also consists of formatting tags, but CSS is superior to HTML because it provides more attributes than HTML. Hence the appearance is much better.
-          CSS coding is compatible with most of the devices such as, PDAs, Computers, Tablets, mobile phones etc.
The rules applied on the elements are responsible for how the content of those elements should appear. The CSS rule is made up of two parts:
-          The selector indicates which element/set of elements to be decorated. If it has to be applied to more than one element, you can separate a list of elements with the comma.
-          The declaration indicates how the elements in the selector should appear.
Example:
Applying values of the attributes to the selected elements in CSS is the same as that of the HTML code but in HTML we need to write and apply the same property and its value on every occurrence of the element. In CSS once it is written it can be applied to the element on every occurrence.
The style definitions are normally saved in external .css files. With this file you can change the look of the entire website. If there are multiple declarations, you can separate them with the semicolon as shown below.
H1{color:red;font-family:verdana;}
Let’s have a look at the sample CSS program.
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Sample CSS Demo</TITLE>
<STYLE>
BODY{
background-color:yellow;
}
h1{
color:blue;
}
p{
font-family:Bookman Old Style
font-size:15px;
}
</STYLE>
</HEAD>
<BODY>
<H1>First CSS Sample Page</H1>
<P>This is a Paragraph<P>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Here, BODY, H1 and P are selectors, and the properties and their values written inside the curly braces are declarations. Type the above code in any one of the editors like, notepad, notepad++ or vs code. In notepad as a programmer you need to remember the names of the properties of each element, whereas, in vs code, it shows the properties associated with each element and values associated with each property. If you use vs code to write the code it makes your life easy. For your easy understanding I am using a notepad.
Type the above css code in notepad and save it with the name Sample CSS Demo.html and open the file to get the above output.
CSS Selectors: The selectors are used to select the HTML elements we want to style.
There are five types of CSS selectors as listed below.
-          Simple selectors
-          Combinator selectors
-          Pseudo-class selectors
-          Pseudo-elements selectors
-          Attribute selectors
Element selector: it selects the HTML elements based on the element name as shown in the following example.
<!--CSS code to illustrate all <p> elements are center-aligned, with a blue text color-->
<html>
<head><title>Paragraph Alignment</title>
<style>
p {
text-align: center;
color: blue;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<p>This is the paragraph that is center aligned and blue in color</p>
<p id="paragraph">This is another paragraph which is center aligned with the blue color</p>
<p>The third paragraph is also set with the same attributes</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
The CSS id selector: The id of an element must be unique, so the id selector is used to select one unique element. To select an element with id, use a hash (#) character, followed by the id of the element.
Example:
<html>
<head><title>id selector demo</title>
<style>
#paragraph {
text-align: center;
color: blue;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<p id="paragraph">This is a paragraph with id selector</p>
<p>This paragraph doesn't have a style effect.</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
The CSS class selector: the class selector selects HTML elements with a specific class attribute. To select elements with a specific class, write a period (.) character, followed by the class name.
Example:
<html>
<head><title>Class Selector Demo</title>
<style>
.cent {
text-align: center;
color: blue;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h1 class="cent">This is a Blue and center-aligned heading</h1>
<p class="cent">This is a Blue and center-aligned paragraph.</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
In this example both the paragraph and the headings applied the same formatting style. It is also specified that only specific HTML elements should be affected by a class. the above code can be modified as shown in the following example.
<html>
<head><title>Class Selector with Specific element</title>
<style>
p.cent {
text-align: center;
color: blue;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h1 class="cent">This heading is intact.</h1>
<p class="cent">This paragraph is blue in color and center-aligned.</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
HTML elements can also refer to more than one class. In the following example, two different classes called cent and lar as shown below.
Example:
<html>
<head><title>Two Class Selectors</title>
<style>
p.cent {
text-align: center;
color: red;
}
p.large {
font-size: 100%;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h1 class="cent">This heading is intact</h1>
<p class="cent">This paragraph will be red and center-aligned.</p>
<p class="cent lar">This paragraph will be red, center-aligned, and in a large font-size.</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
The Universal Selector: The Universal selector is denoted by * character that selects all HTML elements.
Example:
<html>
<head><title>Universal Selector Demo</title>
<style>
* {
text-align: center;
color: blue;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h1>The header applies css style</h1>
<p>This paragraph is also affected by universal selector</p>
<p id="para1">This paragraph is also affected with the style in spite of having id selector</p>
<p>This is another paragraph</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Grouping selector: It selects all the HTML elements with the same style definitions. Instead of defining the same style to different elements separately as shown below.
h1 {
  text-align: center;
  color: red;
}
h2 {
  text-align: center;
  color: red;
}
p {
  text-align: center;
  color: red;
}
We can club together and apply the style to all the elements separated by comma as shown in the following example.
<html>
<head><title>Grouping Selector Demo</title>
<style>
h1, h2, p {
text-align: center;
color: red;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h1>This is heading 1</h1>
<h2>This is heading 2</h2>
<p>This is a paragraph.</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
The following table lists all the Simple Selectors
Selector
Example
#id
#head1
.class
.para
element.class
p.para
*
*
element
p
element,element,…
H1,P
Ways to insert CSS:
There are three ways to insert CSS style sheet:
-          External CSS
-          Internal CSS
-          Inline CSS
External CSS: With the help of one external CSS file we can change the entire appearance of the webpage. Every HTML page must include a reference to the external style sheet inside the <link> element, inside the head section. Let’s have an example of an external CSS style sheet file as follows.
<html>
<head><title>External Style Sheet Demo</title>
<link rel="stylesheet" href="styleme.css">
</head>
<body>
<h1>This is a heading 1</h1>
<p>This is a paragraph.</p>
</body>
</html>
The following external style sheet is to be written in another notepad and save it as styleme.css and this CSS file contains the following code. (It is written as per our requirement you can change it as per user’s requirement).
body{
background-color:red;
}
p{
color:white;
}
Output:
Internal CSS: This is used when one single HTML page has a unique style. Internal style sheets are commonly written inside the <HEAD> section of an HTML page as shown in the following example.
<html>
<head><title>Internal CSS Demo</title>
<style>
body {
background-color: red;
}
h1 {
color: white;
margin-left: 40px;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h1>This is a heading 1.</h1>
<p>This is a paragraph.</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Inline CSS: It is used to apply a unique style for a single element. Inline style is added to the relevant element as shown in the following example.
<html>
<body>
<head><title>Inline CSS Demo</title></head>
<h1 style="color:blue;text-align:center;background-color:yellow">This is a heading 1.</h1>
<p style="color:red;background-color:lightblue">This is a paragraph.</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Multiple style sheets: when we use multiple sheets and define some properties for the same selector then the property that appears last is applied as shown in the following example.
An external styleme.css sheet consists of the following code.
body{
background-color:cyan;
}
p{
color:white;
}
h1{
color:blue;
}
And the internal style sheet consists of the following code.
h1 {
color: maroon;
}
If the internal style sheet is defined after an external style sheet, the <h1> element will be maroon.
<html>
<head><title>Multiple Stylesheet Demo</title>
<link rel="stylesheet" type="text/css" href="styleme.css">
<style>
h1 {
color: maroon;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h1>This is a heading 1</h1>
<p>The output of this document is a combination of an external stylesheet, and internal style sheets</p>
</body>
</html>
Here in this code, an external style sheet is before an internal style sheet, hence the following output.
If the internal style sheet is defined before an external style sheet, the <h1> elements will be blue as shown in the following example.
<html>
<head><title>Multiple Style Sheet when Internal Style sheet before external</title>
<style>
h1 {
color: maroon;
}
</style>
<link rel="stylesheet" type="text/css" href="styleme.css">
</head>
<body>
<h1>This is a heading 1</h1>
<p>The output of this document is a combination of an external stylesheet, and internal style sheets</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Cascading order: In cascading order we will understand what style will be used when there is more than one style specified for an HTML element? All the styles in a page will cascade into a new virtual stylesheet where the highest priority is for inline style. And the remaining priorities are listed as follows.
  1. Inline style sheet.
  2. External and internal style sheet
  3. Browser default.
An inline style sheet has the highest priority, and will override the other two style sheets. Let’s have an example.
<html>
<head><title>Cascading Order Demo</title>
<link rel="stylesheet" type="text/css" href="styleme.css">
<style>
body {
background-color: yellow;
}
</style>
</head>
<body style="background-color: red">
<h1>Cascading Style Sheet into One</h1>
<p>The background color of the page is set with inline, internal, and also with an external CSS.</p>
<p>experiment by removing the inline first, then the internal, then the external CSS.</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Comments in CSS: Comments are used as a documentation section for your source code. The comments are helpful to express the purpose of the code. It makes the other programmer understand the purpose of the code. It is textual information that tells us what the code does. Comments are ignored by the browsers. A CSS comment is placed inside the <style> element start with /* and ends with */ which is a multiline comment, used to comment on more than one line.
Example: /* Set a header color red */
H1{
color:red;
}
It is not necessary to mention the comments at the top; it can be written anywhere as shown in the following example.
H1{
color:red; /* setting text color of <H1> element to red */
}
Here, in this example we are telling that the text color of header 1 is set to red.
CSS Colors:
Colors are specified using predefined color names or RGB,HEX,HSL,RGBA, HSLA values.
Color names: in CSS, a color can be specified by using a predefined color name such as, aqua, blue, brown, cadetblue, tomato, chocolate, crimson, red, yellow etc. there are 140 varieties of colors.
Background color: background color is a property of a style element to set different background colors to the text or background colors to the browser window. Let’s set the background color for HTML elements.
<html>
<head><title>Background Color Demo</title></head>
<body>
<h1 style="background-color:DodgerBlue;">This is a header 1</h1>
<p style="background-color:Tomato;">
Sample text to test</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Text Color: You can set text color using an attribute of style tag color as shown in the following example.
<html>
<head><title>Text Color Demo</title></head>
<body>
<h3 style="color:red;">This text is Red in color</h3>
<p style="color:DodgerBlue;">This text is DodgerBlue in color</p>
<p style="color:Green;">This text is Green in color</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Border Color: Border color can be applied using the border property of <style> element as shown in the following example.
<html>
<body>
<head><title>Border Color Demo</title></head>
<h1 style="border: 2px solid blue;">Solid Blue Border</h1>
<h1 style="border: 2px dotted violet;">Dotted Violet Border</h1>
<h1 style="border: 2px solid green;">Solid Green Border</h1>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Color values: colors can be specified using RGB, HEX,HSL,RGBA and HSLA values.
RGB Colors: An RGB color value represents Red, Green and Blue. It is represented as, rgb(red, green, blue). Each of these colors ranges from 0 to 255.
Example: rgb(255,0,0) displays red because red is set to its highest value (255) and other two colors are set to 0. To display black, set all parameters to rgb(0,0,0).
To display white, set all color parameter to rgb(255,255,255). You can experiment by changing the values of RGB as shown in the following example.
<html>
<head><title>RGB Color Demo</title></head>
<body>
<h1 style="background-color:rgb(255, 20, 40);">rgb(255, 20, 40)</h1>
<h1 style="background-color:rgb(150, 200, 255);">rgb(150, 200, 255)</h1>
<h1 style="background-color:rgb(0, 255, 255);">rgb(0, 255, 255)</h1>
<h1 style="background-color:rgb(120, 130, 140);">rgb(120, 130, 140)</h1>
</body>
</html>
Output:
RGBA values: These values are the extension of RGB color values with an alpha specifying the opacity for a color.
rgba(red,green,blue,alpha)
Note: The alpha parameter is a number between 0.0 (fully transparent) and 1.0 (not transparent).
Example:
<html>
<head><title>RGBA Color Demo</title></head>
<body>
<h1 style="background-color:rgba(255, 0, 0, 0);">rgba(255, 0, 0, 0)</h1>
<h1 style="background-color:rgba(255, 0, 0, 0.2);">rgba(255, 0, 0, 0.2)</h1>
<h1 style="background-color:rgba(255, 0, 0, 0.4);">rgba(255, 0, 0, 0.4)</h1>
<h1 style="background-color:rgba(255, 0, 0, 0.6);">rgba(255, 0, 0, 0.6)</h1>
<h1 style="background-color:rgba(255, 0, 0, 0.8);">rgba(255, 0, 0, 0.8)</h1>
<h1 style="background-color:rgba(255, 0, 0, 1);">rgba(255, 0, 0, 1)</h1>
</body>
</html>
Output:
HEX colors: A hexadecimal color is specified with #RRGGBB, where RR represents Red, GG represents Green and BB represents Blue color. These values range between 0 to 255. Let’s have an example.
<html>
<head><title>Hex Color Demo</title></head>
<body>
<h1 style="background-color:#ff00ff;">#ff00ff</h1>
<h1 style="background-color:#000099;">#000099</h1>
<h1 style="background-color:#12ff34;">#12ff34</h1>
<h1 style="background-color:#e5f5a5;">#e5f5a5</h1>
<h1 style="background-color:#aa1136;">#aa1136</h1>
<h1 style="background-color:#984536;">#984536</h1>
</body>
</html>
Output:
3 Digit Hex Value: You might have seen a 3 digit hex value, this value is a shorthand for some 6 digit hex values.
Syntax:
#rgb
Here, r represents Red, g represents Green and b represents Blue with the values ranging from 0 to f. This type of 3 digit hex value can be used when both the values of RR, GG and BB are the same. Example, #001122, this 6 digit hex code can be written in 3 digit hex code as, #012.
Let’s have an example,
<html>
<head><title>3 Digit Hex Value Demo</title>
<style>
body {
background-color: #012; /* same as #001122 */
}
h1 {
color: #feb; /* same as #ffeebb */
}
p {
color: #bed; /* same as #bbeedd */
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h1>3-digit Hex Code</h1>
<p>This is a paragraph.</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
HSL Colors: HSL stands for hue, saturation and lightness. This is represented as,
hsl(hue, saturation, lightness)
hue is a degree on the color wheel from 0 to 360. 0 is red, 120 is green, and 240 is blue.
Saturation is a percentage value, 0% means a shade of gray, and 100% is the full color.
Lightness is also a percentage, )% is black, 50% is neither light nor dark, 100% is white.
Example:
<html>
<head><title>HSL Color Demo</title></head>
<body>
<p>HSL Color Scheme<p>
<h1 style="background-color:hsl(10, 50%, 50%);">hsl(10, 50%, 50%)</h1>
<h1 style="background-color:hsl(120, 60%, 50%);">hsl(120, 60%, 50%)</h1>
<h1 style="background-color:hsl(150, 100%, 46%);">hsl(150, 100%, 46%)</h1>
<h1 style="background-color:hsl(255, 78%, 42%);">hsl(255, 78%, 42%)</h1>
<h1 style="background-color:hsl(30, 87%, 50%);">hsl(30, 87%, 50%)</h1>
<h1 style="background-color:hsl(100, 51%, 55%);">hsl(100, 51%, 55%)</h1>
</body>
</html>
Output:
HSLA Value: It is an extension of HSL with an alpha channel – which specifies the opacity of a color.
Syntax:
hsla(hue, saturation, lightness, alpha)
The alpha parameter is a number between 0.0 which is fully transparent and 1.0 which is not transparent.
Example:
<html>
<head><title>HSLA Color Value Demo</title></head>
<body>
<p>HSLA color value.</p>
<h1 style="background-color:hsla(0, 100%, 64%, 0);">hsla(0, 100%, 64%, 0)</h1>
<h1 style="background-color:hsla(0, 100%, 64%, 0.2);">hsla(0, 100%, 64%, 0.2)</h1>
<h1 style="background-color:hsla(0, 100%, 64%, 0.4);">hsla(0, 100%, 64%, 0.4)</h1>
<h1 style="background-color:hsla(0, 100%, 64%, 0.6);">hsla(0, 100%, 64%, 0.6)</h1>
<h1 style="background-color:hsla(0, 100%, 64%, 0.8);">hsla(0, 100%, 64%, 0.8)</h1>
<h1 style="background-color:hsla(0, 100%, 64%, 1);">hsla(0, 100%, 64%, 1)</h1>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Backgrounds: This property is used to set the backgrounds for elements. We can set varieties of background effects like,
-          background-color
-          background-image
-          background-repeat
-          background-attachment
-          background-position
-          background
Background-color: This property specifies the background color of an element. Background color properties can be applied to the background of the webpage and background of the text (any element) as shown in the following example.
<html>
<head><title>Background Color Property Demo</title>
<style>
body {
background-color: blue;
}
h1{
background-color: red;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h1>This header 1 background color is set to red.</h1>
<p>This page has a blue background color.</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Opacity or Transparency: The opacity property is used to specify opacity/transparency of an element. It can take a value from 0.0 to 1.0. lower the value, more the transparency is.
<html>
<head><title>Opacity Demo</title>
<style>
div {
background-color: blue;
}
div.first {
opacity: 0.1;
}
div.second {
opacity: 0.3;
}
div.third {
opacity: 0.6;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<div class="first">
<h1>opacity 0.1</h1>
</div>
<div class="second">
<h1>opacity 0.3</h1>
</div>
<div class="third">
<h1>opacity 0.6</h1>
</div>
<div>
<h1>opacity 1 (default)</h1>
</div>
</body>
</html>
Output:
When opacity property is applied to an element, all of its child elements inherit the same transparency.
Opacity using RGBA: When we don’t want to apply opacity to child elements, use RGBA color values as shown in the following example.
<html>
<head><title>Opacity with RGBA Demo</title>
<style>
div{
background: rgb(0,128,0)
}
div.first {
background: rgba(0, 128, 0, 0.1);
}
div.second {
background: rgba(0, 128, 0, 0.3);
}
div.third {
background: rgba(0, 128, 0, 0.6);
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<div class="first">
<h1>10% opacity</h1>
</div>
<div class="second">
<h1>30% opacity</h1>
</div>
<div class="third">
<h1>60% opacity</h1>
</div>
<div>
<h1>default</h1>
</div>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Background image: The background-image property is used to set background image to an element. By default, the image is repeated so it covers the entire element as shown in the following example.
<html>
<head><title>Adding Background Image Demo</title>
<style>
body {
background-image: url("background image.jpeg");
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h1>This heading is on background image</h1>
</body>
</html>
Output:
The background image can also be set for specific elements, like the <p> element as shown in the following example.
<html>
<head><title>Background Image to P element Demo</title>
<style>
p{
background-image: url("background image.jpeg");
color: white;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<p>The background image can also be set to paragraph element</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Background repeat: by default, background-image property repeats an image both horizontally and vertically. Some images should be repeated only horizontally or vertically as shown in the below program.
<html>
<head><title>Background Image repeat Horizontally and Vertically Demo</title>
<style>
body{
background-image: url("background.jfif");
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<p> Images repeat horizontally and vertically.</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
We can make an image to repeat horizontally, using background-repeat: repeat-x property as shown below.
<html>
<head><title>Background Image repeat Horizontally Demo</title>
<style>
body{
background-image: url("background.jfif");
background-repeat: repeat-x;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<p> Images repeat horizontally. </p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
We can make an image to repeat vertically, using background-repeat: repeat-y property as shown below.
<html>
<head><title>Background Image repeat Vertically Demo</title>
<style>
body{
background-image: url("background.jfif");
background-repeat: repeat-y;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<p> Images repeat vertically.</p>
</body>

Background – shorthand property:
To make the code short, we can write properties in one single property called background.
Instead of writing the following code.
body {

  background-repeat: no-repeat;
  background-position: right top;

-          background-attachment
-          background-position
it's okay to see some property values are missing, as long as the other ones are in this order. Note that we do not use the background-attachment property in the example above, as it does not have a value.
All CSS Background Properties
Property
Description
background
Sets all the background properties in one declaration
background-attachment
Sets whether a background image is fixed or scrolls with the rest of the page

Sets how a background image will be repeated

Specifies the size of the background image(s)
Border style: The border-style property is used to display the various types of border to display.
The various types of borders are,
-          dotted
-          dashed
-          solid
-          double
-          groove
-          ridge
-          inset
-          outset
-          none
-          hidden
The border-style property can have from one to four values, top border, right border, bottom border and the left border. Let’s have an example to demonstrate all of these four values of border.
<HTML>
<HEAD><TITLE>Varieties of Borders Demo</TITLE>
<STYLE>
P.TOP{
border-style: solid none none none;
}
P.RIGHT{
border-style: none solid none none;
}
P.BOTTOM{
border-style: none none solid none;
}
P.LEFT{
border-style: none none none solid;
}
</STYLE>
<HEAD>
<BODY>
<P class="TOP">This is a top border</P>
<P class="RIGHT">This is a right border</p>
<P class="BOTTOM">This is a bottom border</p>
<P class="LEFT">This is a left border</p>
</BODY>
</HTML>
Output:
Note: The order of the sides of a border is TOP, RIGHT, BOTTOM and LEFT.
The following code demonstrate the different types of border styles

How does border-style property work?
If the border-style property has four values:
●      border-style: dotted solid double dashed;
  • top border is dotted
  • right border is solid
  • bottom border is double
  • left border is dashed
If the border-style property has three values:
●      border-style: dotted solid double;
  • top border is dotted
  • right and left borders are solid
  • bottom border is double
If the border-style property has two values:
●      border-style: dotted solid;
  • top and bottom borders are dotted
  • right and left borders are solid
If the border-style property has one value:
●      border-style: dotted;
  • all four borders are dotted
Let’s have an example of border-style.
<html>
<head><title>Individual border sides demo</title>
<style>
body {
text-align: center;
}
/* Four values */
p.four {
border-style: solid dotted double dashed;
}
/* Three values */
p.three {
border-style: dashed solid double;
}
/* Two values */
p.two {
border-style: solid dotted;
}
/* One value */
p.one {
border-style: solid;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h2>Individual Border Sides</h2>
<p class="four">4 different border styles.</p>
<p class="three">3 different border styles.</p>
<p class="two">2 different border styles.</p>
<p class="one">1 border style.</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:

Margins: The margin property is used to create space around elements, outside of any defined borders.
Margin – Individual Sides: It is used to specify margin for each side of an element as below,
-          margin-top
-          margin-right
-          margin-bottom
-          margin-left
These have margin properties can have the following values:
-          auto
-          length
-          %
-          Inherit
The values for these properties, negative values are also allowed.
Example:
<html>
<head><title>Individual Margin Properties Demo</title>
<style>
p {
border: 1px solid black;
margin-top: 100px;
margin-bottom: 100px;
margin-right: 150px;
margin-left: 80px;
background-color: orange;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h2>Individual Margin Properties Demo</h2>
<p>This is a Paragraph.</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Margin- shorthand property: It is possible to set all the margin properties in one property as shown below.
If the margin property has four values:
●      margin: 25px 50px 75px 100px;
  • top margin is 25px
  • right margin is 50px
  • bottom margin is 75px
  • left margin is 100px
If the margin property has three values:
●      margin: 25px 50px 75px;
  • top margin is 25px
  • right and left margins are 50px
  • bottom margin is 75px
If the margin property has two values:
●      margin: 25px 50px;
  • top and bottom margins are 25px
  • right and left margins are 50px
If the margin property has one value:
●      margin: 25px;
  • all four margins are 25px
The auto value: This property of margin makes the elements horizontally center within its container.
The inherit value: The properties inherit from one element to another using a value called inherit.
Let’s take an example to demonstrate all the above properties in the code below.
<html>
<head><title>Margin Shorthand Property</title>
<style>
p.four {
border: 1px solid black;
margin: 25px 50px 75px 100px;
background-color: lightblue;
}
p.three {
border: 1px solid black;
margin: 25px 50px 75px;
background-color: orange;
}
p.two {
border: 1px solid black;
margin: 25px 50px;
background-color: crimson;
}
p.one {
border: 1px solid black;
margin: 25px;
background-color: green;
}
p.auto {
border: 1px solid black;
margin: auto;
background-color: gray;
}
p.inherit {
border: 1px solid black;
margin: inherit;
background-color: tomtato;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h2>The margin shorthand properties</h2>
<p class="four">This is a paragraph with all the four values.</p>
<p class="three">This is a paragraph with the three values.</p>
<p class="two">This is a paragraph with the two values.</p>
<p class="one">This is a paragraph with one value.</p>
<p class="auto">This is a paragraph with auto value.</p>
<p class="inherit">This is a paragraph with inherited value.</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Margin collapse: Top and bottom margins of elements are sometimes collapsed into a single margin that is equal to the largest of the two margins. This is not applicable to left and right margins. Let’s have a code that demonstrates the margin collapse.
<html>
<head><title>Margin Collapse Demo</title>
<style>
p.one {
margin: 0 0 30px 0;
}
p.two {
margin: 20px 0 0 0;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<p class="one">Heading 1</p>
<p class="two">Heading 2</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
In the above example, the <p class=”one”> element has a bottom margin 30px and the <p class=”two”> element has a top margin 20px. We think that the vertical margin between the elements would be 50px (30px+20px). But due to margin collapse, the actual margin ends up being 30px.
Padding: It is used to leave empty space around an element’s content, inside of any defined borders. Padding has following padding properties.
-          padding-top
-          padding-right
-          padding-bottom
-          padding-left
all the padding properties can have the following values:
-          length
-          %
-          Inherit
Negative values for these properties are not allowed.
Padding also has shorthand property as,
If the padding property has four values:
●      padding: 25px 50px 75px 100px;
  • top padding is 25px
  • right padding is 50px
  • bottom padding is 75px
  • left padding is 100px
If the padding property has three values:
●      padding: 25px 50px 75px;
  • top padding is 25px
  • right and left paddings are 50px
  • bottom padding is 75px
If the padding property has two values:
●      padding: 25px 50px;
  • top and bottom paddings are 25px
  • right and left paddings are 50px
If the padding property has one value:
●      padding: 25px;
  • all four paddings are 25px
Example to demonstrate all of the above padding properties.
<html>
<head><title>All Padding Properties Demo</title>
<style>
p.all {
border: 1px solid black;
background-color: lightblue;
padding-top: 50px;
padding-right: 30px;
padding-bottom: 50px;
padding-left: 80px;
}
p.four{
border: 1px solid black;
background-color: lightblue;
padding: 25px 50px 75px 100px;
}
p.three{
border: 1px solid black;
background-color: lightblue;
padding: 25px 50px 75px;
}
p.two{
border: 1px solid black;
background-color: lightblue;
padding: 25px 50px;
}
p.one{
border: 1px solid black;
background-color: lightblue;
padding: 25px;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h2>All padding properties</h2>
<p class="all">This p element has a top padding of 50px, a right padding of 30px, a bottom padding of 50px, and a left padding of 80px.</p>
<p class="four">This p element has a top padding of 25px, a right padding of 50px, a bottom padding of 75px, and a left padding of 100px.</p>
<p class="three">This p element has a top padding of 25px, a right padding of 50px, a bottom padding of 75px, and a left padding of 50px.</p>
<p class="two">This p element has a top padding of 25px, a right padding of 50px, a bottom padding of 25px, and a left padding of 50px.</p>

Padding and element width: The width property specifies the width of the element’s content area. If an element has a specified width, the padding added to that element will be added to the total width of the element. This leads to undesirable results.
<html>
<head><title>Padding and Element WIdth Demo</title>
<style>
p.one {
width: 300px;
background-color: yellow;
}
p.two {
width: 300px;
padding: 25px;
background-color: lightblue;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h2>Padding and element width</h2>
<p class="one">This p is 300px wide.</p>
<br>
<p class="two">The width of this p is 350px, even though it is defined as 300px in the CSS.</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Here, the <p> element is given a width of 300px. However, the actual width of the <p> element will be 350px (300px+25px of left padding+25px of right padding).
To keep the width at 300px, no matter the amount of padding, you can use the box-sizing property. This causes the element to maintain its actual width; if you increase the padding, the available content space will decrease as shown in the following example.
<html>
<head><title>Padding and Element Width with box-sizing Demo</title>
<style>
p.one {
width: 300px;
background-color: yellow;
}
p.two {
width: 300px;
padding: 25px;
box-sizing: border-box;
background-color: lightblue;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h2>Padding and element width with box-sizing property</h2>
<p class="one">This p is 300px wide.</p>
<br>
<p class="two">The width of this p is 350px, even though it is defined as 300px in the CSS.</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Height/width: height and width properties have the following values:
-          auto – the browser calculates the height and width.
-          Length – length defined the height and width in px, cm etc.
-          % - defines the height and width in percent of the containing block.
-          Initial – sets the height and width to its default values.
-          Inherit – the height and width will be inherited from its parent value.
Example to demonstrate height and width of an element.
<html>
<head><title>Height and Width Demo</title>
<style>
div {
height: 200px;
width: 500;
background-color: orange;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h2>Set the height and width of an element</h2>
<div>This div element has a height of 200px and a width of 500.</div>
</body>
</html>
Output:
You give a try for other values of height and width of an element.
Note that the height and width do not include padding, borders or margins. It sets the height and width of the area inside the padding, border and margin of the element.
Max-width: This property is used to set maximum width.
<html>
<head><title>max-width demo</title>
<style>
div {
max-width: 500px;
height: 100px;
background-color: crimson;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h2>Set the max-width of an element</h2>
<div>This div element has a height of 100px and a max-width of 500px.</div>
<p>Resize the browser window to see the effect.</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
If both width and max-width properties are used, the value of the width property is larger than the max-width property; the max-width property will be used and the width property will be ignored.
Box model: the box model consists of content surrounded by padding surrounded by border surrounded by margin as shown in the following figure.
Example to demonstrate the box model
<html>
<head><title>Box Model Demo</title>
<style>
div {
background-color: lightgrey;
width: 300px;
border: 15px solid red;
padding: 50px;
margin: 20px;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h2>The Box Model</h2>
<div>This text is the content of the box. We have added a 50px padding, 20px margin and a 15px red border. </div>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Outline: A line drawn outside the element’s border as shown in the following figure.
Outline has the following properties.
-          outline-style
-          outline-color
-          outline-width
-          outline-offset
-          outline
outline-style has the following values:
-          dotted
-          dashed
-          solid
-          double
-          groove
-          ridge
-          inset
-          outset
-          none
-          hidden
example to demonstrate values of outline properties.
<html>
<head><title>Outline Property Demo</title>
<style>
p {outline-color:blue}
p.dotted {outline-style: dotted;}
p.dashed {outline-style: dashed;}
p.solid {outline-style: solid;}
p.double {outline-style: double;}
p.groove {outline-style: groove;}
p.ridge {outline-style: ridge;}
p.inset {outline-style: inset;}
p.outset {outline-style: outset;}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h2>The outline-style Property</h2>
<p class="dotted">A dotted outline</p>
<p class="dashed">A dashed outline</p>
<p class="solid">A solid outline</p>
<p class="double">A double outline</p>
<p class="groove">A groove outline.</p>
<p class="ridge">A ridge outline.</p>
<p class="inset">An inset outline.</p>
<p class="outset">An outset outline.</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Outline width: outline-width property can have the following values:
-          thin (1px)
-          medium (3px)
-          thick (5px)
-          a specific size (in px, pt, cm, em etc)
example to demonstrate all the values of the outline property.
<html>
<head><title>Outline Width Demo</title>
<style>
p.thin {
border: 2px solid black;
outline-style: solid;
outline-color: red;
outline-width: thin;
}
p.medium {
border: 2px solid black;
outline-style: solid;
outline-color: red;
outline-width: medium;
}
p.thick {
border: 2px solid black;
outline-style: solid;
outline-color: red;
outline-width: thick;
}
p.specific {
border: 2px solid black;
outline-style: solid;
outline-color: red;
outline-width: 4px;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h2>The outline-width Property</h2>
<p class="thin">A thin outline.</p>
<p class="medium">A medium outline.</p>
<p class="thick">A thick outline.</p>
<p class="specific">A 4px thick outline.</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Outline color can be of name, HEX, RGB, HSL. You give a try for these types of colors.

-          outline-width
-          outline-style
-          outline-color
example to demonstrate all the properties of outline.

<p class="thick">A thick ridge crimson outline.</p>
</body>

Output:
Outline offset: The outline-offset property adds space between an outline and the edge or border of an element. The space between an element and its outline is transparent.
Example to demonstrate outline-offset property.
<html>
<head><title>Outline Offset Demo</title>
<style>
p {
margin: 20px;
border: 2px solid green;
outline: 2px solid orange;
outline-offset: 10px;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h2>The outline-offset Property</h2>
<p>This paragraph has an outline 10px outside the border edge.</p>
</body>
</html>

CHAPTER 9
TEXT FORMATTING
CSS text has a lot of formatting properties for text. Let’s begin with text color, text color can be set using a color name, a HEX value or an RGB value. Let’s have an example to demonstrate text color.
<html>
<head><title>Text Color Property Demo</title>
<style>
body {
color: red;
}
h1 {
color: green;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h1>This is heading 1</h1>
<p>This paragraph is red in color as it is defined in the body selector.</p>
<p>one more paragraph that sets the same color as that of the body selector.</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Background color: we can also set background color for the text as well as the other elements, using background-color property as shown in the following example.
<html>
<head><title>Background text and body Color Demo</title>
<style>
body {
background-color: pink;
color: blue;
}
h1 {
background-color: lightblue;
color: red;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h1>This Heading has red text color and light blue background color</h1>
<p>This page has a pink background color and blue text color</p>
<p>Another paragraph.</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Text alignment: the text-align property is used to set the horizontal alignment of a text. A text can be left, centered, right, or justified. Let’s have an example to demonstrate all the alignments. The text is left aligned by default.
<html>
<head><title>Text Align Demo</title>
<style>
h1 {
text-align: center;
}
h2 {
text-align: left;
}
h3 {
text-align: right;
}
p {
border: 1px solid black;
padding: 5px;
width=200px;
height=200px;
text-align: justify;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h1>Heading 1 (center)</h1>
<h2>Heading 2 (left)</h2>
<h3>Heading 3 (right)</h3>
<p>Hello there! My name is Hanumanth Ladwa, I have been teaching since 2008 from the day of my masters degree. And I have more than a decade of teaching experience. Till now I have taught thousands of students across the globe.</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Notice that, when text-align: justify is set, each line is stretched to the width of the container.
Text direction: The direction and Unicode-bidi properties can be used to change the text direction of an element as shown in the following example.
<html>
<head><title>Text Direction</title>
<style>
p.name {
direction: rtl;
unicode-bidi: bidi-override;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<p>My name is Hanumanth Ladwa.</p>
<p class="name">My name is Hanumanth Ladwa.</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:

<p>An <img class="b" src="MS.gif" width="9" height="9"> image with a text-top alignment.</p>

Text transformation: This property is used to change uppercase or lowercase or capitalize the first letter of each word as shown in the following example.

Output:
Text indentation: Text indentation property is used to specify the indentation of the first line of a text.
Letter spacing: This is used to provide the space between the characters.
Line height: This property is used to provide the space between lines.
Word spacing: This property is used to provide space between the words in a text.
White space: This property is used to provide how white space inside an element is handled.
Let’s have an example that shows all of these properties.
<html>
<head><title>Text Spacing Demo</title>
<style>
p.indent {
text-indent: 50px;
}
p.spacing {
text-spacing: 4px;
}
p.height {
line-height: 0.5;
}
p.wspace {
word-spacing: 6px;
}
p.whitespace {
white-space: nowrap;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h4>Text Indent</h4>
<p class="indent">My name is Hanumanth Ladwa. Completed a Masters degree in Computer Science and taught thousands of students both online and offline. I have more than a decade of teaching experience in various subjects.</p>
<h4>Letter Spacing</h4>
<p class="spacing">My name is Hanumanth Ladwa. Completed a Masters degree in Computer Science and taught thousands of students both online and offline. I have more than a decade of teaching experience in various subjects.</p>
<h4>Line Height</h4>
<p class="height">My name is Hanumanth Ladwa. Completed a Masters degree in Computer Science and taught thousands of students both online and offline. I have more than a decade of teaching experience in various subjects.</p>
<h4>Word Spacing</h4>
<p class="wspace">My name is Hanumanth Ladwa. Completed a Masters degree in Computer Science and taught thousands of students both online and offline. I have more than a decade of teaching experience in various subjects.</p>
<h4>White Space</h4>
<p class="whitespace">My name is Hanumanth Ladwa. Completed a Masters degree in Computer Science and taught thousands of students both online and offline. I have more than a decade of teaching experience in various subjects.</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Text shadow: This property adds shadow to text. We can specify horizontal and vertical shadow effects. We can change the color of shadow and even can give a blur effect as shown in the following example.
<html>

h1 {
text-shadow: 2px 2px;
}

}

text-shadow: 2px 2px 4px red;
}

<h3>Text-shadow 2px 2px red color with blur effect</h3>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Font family: Choosing a font face is very crucial for your website, because it creates an impact on readers of your website. We have various types of fonts such as Times New Roman, Georgia, Garamond, Arial, Verdana etc. To use any of the font faces we use font-family property. We will write font names as a fallback system, to ensure maximum compatibility between browsers and operating systems. Start with the font you want, and end with a generic font family to let the browser pick a similar font in the generic family, if no other fonts are available. Let’s have an example.
<html>
<head><title>Font Family Demo</title>
<style>
.p1 {
font-family: "Times New Roman", Times, serif;
}
.p2 {
font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif;
}
.p3 {
font-family: "Lucida Console", "Courier New", monospace;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h1>CSS font-family</h1>
<p class="p1">This paragraph is shown in the Times New Roman font.</p>
<p class="p2">This paragraph is shown in the Arial font.</p>
<p class="p3">This paragraph is shown in the Lucida Console font.</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Font style: This property has three values:
-          normal
-          italic
-          oblique
Font weight: This property is used to change the weight of a font.
Font variant: This property specifies whether or not a text should be displayed in a small-caps font.
Let’s have a program to illustrate all of these properties.
<html>
<head><title>Font Style Demo</title>
<style>
p.normal {
font-style: normal;
}
p.italic {
font-style: italic;
}
p.oblique {
font-style: oblique;
}
p.weightnormal {
font-weight: normal;
}
p.light {
font-weight: lighter;
}
p.thick {
font-weight: bold;
}
p.thicker {
font-weight: 600;
}
p.fontnormal {
font-variant: normal;
}
p.small{
font-variant: small-caps;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<p class="normal">This is a paragraph in normal style.</p>
<p class="italic">This is a paragraph in italic style.</p>
<p class="oblique">This is a paragraph in oblique style.</p>
<p class="weightnormal">This is a paragraph in normal weight style.</p>
<p class="light">This is a paragraph in a lighter weight style.</p>
<p class="thick">This is a paragraph in thick style.</p>
<p class="thicker">This is a paragraph in thicker style.</p>
<p class="fontnormal">This is a paragraph in font normal style.</p>
<p class="small">This is a paragraph in small style.</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Font size: This property sets the size of the text. We can set the font size in pixels, em and percentage.
Responsive font size: The text size can be set with a viewport width which is denoted as vw unit. The text size will follow the size of the browser window.
Let’s have an example to show the use of all the above font properties.
<html>
<head><title>Font Size Demo</title>
<style>
p.pixel {
font-size: 50px;
}
p.em {
font-size: 1em;
}
p.percentage {
font-size: 14%;
}
p.responsive {
font-size: 10vw;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<p class="pixel">This paragraph uses pixel size.</p>
<p class="em">This paragraph uses em size.</p>
<p class="percemtage">This paragraph uses percentage size.</p>
<p class="responsive">This paragraph uses responsive size, resize browser window.</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Google fonts: if we don’t want to use any of the standard fonts in HTML, you can use google fonts. In order to use google fonts, use a special style sheet link in the <head> section as shown in the following example.
<html>
<head><title>Google Font Demo</title>
<link rel="stylesheet" href="https://fonts.googleapis.com/css?family=Literata">
<style>
body {
font-family: "Literata", sans-serif;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h1>Literata Font</h1>
<p>This paragraph is written in Literata font face.</p>
<p>123456790</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Google fonts can also be styled using CSS and by enabling font effects as shown in the following example.
<html>
<head>
<link rel="stylesheet" href="https://fonts.googleapis.com/css?family=Literata&effect=neon|outline|shadow-multiple|fire">
<style>
body {
font-family: "Literata", sans-serif;
font-size: 30px;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h1 class="font-effect-neon">Neon Effect</h1>
<h1 class="font-effect-outline">Outline Effect</h1>
<h1 class="font-effect-shadow-multiple">Multiple Shadow Effect</h1>
<h1 class="font-effect-fire">Fire</h1>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Font shorthand property: With the help of font shorthand property, it is possible to specify all the individual font properties in one property. It uses the following shorthand property for:
-          font-style
-          font-variant
-          font-weight
-          font-size/line-height
-          font-family
Let’s take a look at the following example that demonstrates font shorthand property.
<html>
<head><title>Font Property</title>
<style>
p.a {
font:italic bold 30px Georgia, serif;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h1>The font Property</h1>
<p class="a">This is a paragraph. The font size is set to 30 pixels, and the font family is Georgia.</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
CSS Links: In CSS, links can be styled with any CSS property such as color, font-family, background, etc. links can be styled differently depending on what state they are in. following are the four links states:
-          a:link – a normal, unvisited link.
-          a:visited – a link the user has visited.
-          a:hover – a link when the user moves over it.
-          a:active – a link the moment it is clicked.
Text decoration: This property is used to remove underlines from links.
Background color: This property is used to specify a background color for links.
Link buttons: Here, links can be displayed as boxes by combining several properties.
Example that demonstrate CSS Links
<html>
<head><title>CSS Links Demo</title>
<style>
a:link, a:visited {
background-color: white;
color: black;
border: 2px solid green;
padding: 10px 20px;
text-align: center;
text-decoration: none;
display: inline-block;
}
a:hover, a:active {
background-color: green;
color: white;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<a href="https://www.puiicomputerscience.com" target="_blank">Welcome to my Website!</a>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Tables: The appearance of an HTML table can be greatly improved with CSS using various properties as follows:
Table borders: The border property is used to specify table borders.
Full-width table: To make a table widened across the width of the window, use width: 100% to the <table> element.
Collapse table borders: The border-collapse property is used to set whether the table borders should be collapsed into a single border.
If we want only a border around the table, use the border property for the <table> element.
<html>
<head><title>Border To a Table Demo</title>
<style>
table, th, td {
border: 1px solid black;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h2>Add a border to a table:</h2>
<table>
<tr>
<th>Firstname</th>
<th>Lastname</th>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Hanumanth</td>
<td>Ladwa</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Roopa</td>
<td>Niranjan</td>
</tr>
</table>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Table width and height: table width and height can be altered using width and height properties of the <table> element.
To create a table that should only span half the page, use width: 50%.
In the following example both of the above two properties are covered.
<html>
<head><title>Table Border width and height property Demo</title>
<style>
table, th, td {
border: 1px solid black;
}
table {
border-collapse: collapse;
width: 50%;
}
</style>
</head>

<th>Lastname</th>

<td>Ladwa</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Roopa</td>
<td>Niranjan</td>
</tr>
</table>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Horizontal and vertical alignment: The text-align property is used to align the text horizontally like, left, center or right of the content in <th> or <td>. By default, the content of <th> elements are center-aligned and the content of <td> elements are left-aligned.
The vertical align property is used to set the vertical alignment like top, middle or bottom of the content in <th> or <td>. By default, the vertical alignment of the content in a table is middle for both <th> and <td> elements.
The following example illustrates horizontal alignments.
<html>
<head><title>Table Text Align Property</title>
<style>
table, td, th {
border: 1px solid black;
}
table {
border-collapse: collapse;
width: 100%;
}
td.one {
text-align: center;
}
td.two {
text-align: left;
}
td.three {
text-align: right;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h2>The text horizontal align Property</h2>
<p>Text aligned on center</p>
<table>
<tr>
<th>Firstname</th>
<th>Lastname</th>
</tr>
<tr>
<td class="one">Hanumanth</td>
<td class="one">Ladwa</td>
</table>
<p>Text aligned on left</p>
<table>
<tr>
<th>Firstname</th>
<th>Lastname</th>
</tr>
<tr>
<td class="two">Roopa</td>
<td class="two">Niranjan</td>
</table>
<p>text aligned on right</p>
<table>
<tr>
<th>Firstname</th>
<th>Lastname</th>
</tr>
<tr>
<td class="three">Deepa</td>
<td class="three">Habib</td>
</table>
</body>
</html>
Output:
The following example illustrates vertical alignments.
<html>
<head><title>Table Text vertical Align Property</title>

</head>

Hoverable table: To highlight a table row on mouse over, use :hover selector as shown in the following example.
<html>
<head><title>Hoverable Table Demo</title>
<style>
table {
border-collapse: collapse;
width: 100%;
}
th, td {
padding: 8px;
text-align: left;
border-bottom: 1px solid black;
}
tr:hover {background-color: yellow;}
</style>
</head>
<body>

<th>Last Name</th>

<td>Niranjan</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Deepa</td>
<td>Habib</td>
</tr>
</table>
</body>
</html>

The Display Property: This property is used to control layout. The default display value for most elements is block or inline.
Block level elements: a block level element always starts on a new line and stretches out to the left and right. Examples of block-level elements:
-          <div>
-          <h1> - <h6>
-          <p>
-          <form>
-          <header>
-          <footer>
-          <section>
Inline elements: An inline element does not start on a new line and stretches to the width as necessary. Example of inline elements:
-          <span>
-          <a>
-          <img>
Override the default display value: Changing an inline element to a block element, or vice versa, can be useful for making the page look a specific way. A common example is making inline <li> elements for horizontal menus:
<html>
<head><title>Display Links as Horizontal Menu</title>
<style>
li {
display: inline;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<p>Display links as horizontal menu:</p>
<ul>
<li><a href="#" target="_blank">HTML</a></li>
<li><a href="#" target="_blank">CSS</a></li>
<li><a href="#" target="_blank">JavaScript</a></li>
</ul>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Let’s have another example that displays <a> elements as block elements.
<html>
<head><title>Display Links as block elements</title>
<style>
li {
display: block;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<p>Display Links as block elements</p>
<ul>
<li><a href="#" target="_blank">HTML</a></li>
<li><a href="#" target="_blank">CSS</a></li>
<li><a href="#" target="_blank">JavaScript</a></li>
</ul>
</body>
</html>
Output:
display: none; is used for hiding an element and the page will be displayed as if the element is not there.
visibility: hidden; also hides an element.
Examples that demonstrate display: none and visibility: hidden.
<html>
<head><title>Display and Visibility demo</title>
<style>
p.none {
display: none;
}
p.visibility {
visibility: hidden;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<p>This is a visible paragraph</p>
<p class="none">This is a hidden paragraph.</p>
<p class="visibility">This is a visibility hidden paragraph</p>
<p>Notice that the p element with display: none; does not take up any space.</p>
<p>Notice that the p element with visibility: hidden; take up space.</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
width and max-width: A block level element stretches out to both the sides. When we set width, it will prevent it from stretching out to the edges of its container. When the browser window is smaller than the width of the element, the browser then adds a horizontal scrollbar to the page.
To prevent this situation, use max-width property when making a site usable on small devices. Examples that follow demonstrate both width and max-width property.
<html>
<head><title>Width and  Max-width Demo</title>
<style>
div.one {
width: 500px;
margin: auto;
border: 3px solid red;
}
div.two {
max-width: 500px;
margin: auto;
border: 3px solid red;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h2>CSS width and  Max-width</h2>
<div class="one">This div element has width: 500px;</div>
<br>
<div class="two">This div element has max-width: 500px;</div>
<p><strong>Tip:</strong> Drag the browser window to smaller than 500px wide, to see the difference between
the two divs!</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
The position property: The element can be positioned using position property. The positions could be static, relative, fixed, absolute or sticky.
Static: It is always positioned according to the normal flow of the page. HTML elements are positioned static by default.
Relative: It is used to position relative to its normal position.
Fixed: It is used to place the element in the same position even if the page is scrolled.
Absolute: It is positioned relative to the nearest positioned ancestor instead of positioned relative to the viewport, like fixed. If it has no positioned ancestors, it uses the document body, and moves along with page scrolling.
Sticky: It is positioned based on the user’s scroll position. internet explorer does not support sticky positioning. Safari requires a -webkit- prefix. In the example below I have opened the file in firefox mozilla browser.
Examples that demonstrate all of the above position properties.
<html>
<head><title>Position Demo</title>
<style>
div.static {
position: static;
border: 3px solid #73AD21;
}
div.relative {
position: relative;
left: 30px;
border: 3px solid #73AD21;
}
div.fixed {
position: fixed;
bottom: 0;
right: 0;
width: 300px;
border: 3px solid #73AD21;
}
div.absolute {
position: absolute;
top: 80px;
right: 0;
width: 200px;
height: 100px;
border: 3px solid #73AD21;
}
div.sticky {
position: -webkit-sticky; /* Safari */
position: sticky;
top: 0;
background-color: green;
border: 2px solid #4CAF50;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h4>position: static;</h4>
<div class="static">
This div element has position: static;
</div>
<h4>position: relative;</h4>
<div class="relative">
This div element has position: relative;
</div>
<h4>position: fixed;</h4>
<div class="fixed">
This div element has position: fixed;
</div>
<h4>position: absolute;</h4>
<div class="absolute">
This div element has position: absolute;
</div>
<h4>position: sticky;</h4>
<div class="sticky">
This is a sticky position;
</div>
<p>Here I am going to use the demo text!

Overlapping elements: in order to overlap the elements positioned, use z-index property to specify the stack order of an element that specifies which element should be placed in front of or behind the others. An element with greater stack order is always in front of an element with a lower stack order.
<html>
<head><title>Overlapping Elements Demo</title>
<style>
img {
position: absolute;
left: 0px;
top: 0px;
z-index: -1;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h1>My name is Hanumanth Ladwa</h1>
<img src="Hanumanth Ladwa.png" width="140" height="140">
</body>
</html>
Output:
The element positioned last will be shown if the elements are not positioned with z-index.

font-size: 18px;

bottom: 8px;
right: 16px;
font-size: 18px;
}
.bottomleft {
position: absolute;
bottom: 8px;
left: 16px;
font-size: 18px;
}

position: absolute;
top: 50%;
width: 100%;
text-align: center;
font-size: 18px;
}
img {
width: 100%;
height: 100%;
opacity: 0.3;
}
</style>

Overflow: This property controls too big to fit into an area. It specifies whether to clip the content or to add horizontal and vertical scrollbars. This property has the following values.
-          Visible – this is the default value. The overflow is not clipped and the content renders outside the element’s box.
<html>
<head><title>Overflow Visible Demo</title>
<style>
div {
background-color: lightgray;
width: 200px;
height: 50px;
border: 1px solid black;
overflow: visible;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>

<div>Lorem ipsum dolor, sit amet consectetur adipisicing elit. Nemo voluptates quae eveniet nam quas temporibus, vitae voluptatum doloremque reprehenderit cumque dignissimos nesciunt omnis consectetur animi. Facilis officiis laboriosam incidunt repellat.</div>
</body>
</html>
Output:
-          Hidden – the overflow will be clipped and the rest of the content will be invisible.
<html>
<head><title>Overflow Hidden Demo</title>
<style>
div {
background-color: lightgray;
width: 200px;
height: 50px;
border: 1px solid black;
overflow: hidden;
}
</style>

</body>
</html>
Output:
-          Scroll – the overflow is clipped and the rest of the content will be visible under the scrollbar.

width: 200px;
height: 50px;

Float and clear: float can be used to wrap text around images whereas, clear property specifies what elements can float beside the cleared element and on which side. The float property has the following values.
-          Left
-          Right
-          None
-          Inherit
Example to demonstrate right and left float and the remaining two values left as you to work.
<html>
<head><title>Float Demo</title>
<style>
img.right {
float: right;
}
img.left {
float: left;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h5>Float Right</h5>
<p><img class="right" src="MS.gif" style="margin-left:15px;">
Lorem ipsum dolor, sit amet consectetur adipisicing elit. Nemo voluptates quae eveniet nam quas temporibus, vitae voluptatum doloremque reprehenderit cumque dignissimos nesciunt omnis consectetur animi. Facilis officiis laboriosam incidunt repellat.</p>
<h5>Float Right</h5>
<p><img class="left" src="MS.gif" style="margin-left:15px;">
Lorem ipsum dolor, sit amet consectetur adipisicing elit. Nemo voluptates quae eveniet nam quas temporibus, vitae voluptatum doloremque reprehenderit cumque dignissimos nesciunt omnis consectetur animi. Facilis officiis laboriosam incidunt repellat.</p>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Float next to each other: The div elements will be displayed on top of each other. If we use float:left the elements float next to each other. Let’s take an example.
<html>
<head><title>Float Next to Each Other Demo</title>
<style>
div {
float: left;
padding: 10px;
}
.div1 {
background: red;
}
.div2 {
background: cyan;
}
.div3 {
background: blue;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h2>Float Next To Each Other</h2>
<div class="div1">First Div</div>
<div class="div2">Second Div</div>
<div class="div3">Third Div</div>
</body>
</html>
Output:
The clear property has the following values.
-          None – the element is not pushed below left or right floated.
-          Left – the element is pushed below left floated elements.
-          Right – the element is pushed below right floated elements.
-          Both – the element is pushed below both left and right floated elements.
-          Inherit – the element inherits the clear value from its parent.
<html>
<head><title>Clear Demo</title>
<style>
.div1 {
float: left;
padding: 10px;
border: 3px solid blue;
}
.div2 {
padding: 10px;
border: 3px solid red;
}
.div3 {
float: left;
padding: 10px;
border: 3px solid blue;
}
.div4 {
padding: 10px;
border: 3px solid red;
clear: left;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h2>Without clear</h2>
<div class="div1">div1</div>
<div class="div2">div2 - Notice that div2 is after div1 in the HTML code. However, since div1 floats to the left, the text in div2 flows around div1.</div>
<br><br>
<h2>With clear</h2>
<div class="div3">div3</div>
<div class="div4">div4 - Here, clear: left; moves div4 down below the floating div3. The value "left" clears elements floated to the left. You can also clear "right" and "both".</div>
</body>
</html>
Output:
The Clearfix Hack: when the floated element is bigger than the containing element, it will overflow outside of its container. To solve this problem, we use clearfix as shown in the following example.
<html>
<head><title>Clearfix Demo</title>
<style>
div {
border: 3px solid #4CAF50;
padding: 5px;
}
.img1 {
float: right;
}
.img2 {
float: right;
}
.clearfix {
overflow: auto;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h2>Without Clearfix</h2>
<div>
<img class="img1" src="Hanumanth Ladwa.png" width="170" height="170">
My name is Hanumanth Ladwa. Completed a Masters degree in Computer Science and taught thousands of students both online and offline. I have more than a decade of teaching experience in various subjects.
</div>
<h2 style="clear:right">With Clearfix</h2>
<div class="clearfix">
<img class="img2" src="Hanumanth Ladwa.png" width="170" height="170">
My name is Hanumanth Ladwa. Completed a Masters degree in Computer Science and taught thousands of students both online and offline. I have more than a decade of teaching experience in various subjects.
</div>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Float examples: if we want to add three floating boxes side by side. If the content in the box enlarges the width of each box will break. The box-sizing property allows us to include the padding and border in the box’s total width and height, making sure that the padding stays inside of the box and that it does not break.
<html>
<head><title>Float Example1 Demo</title>
<style>
* {
box-sizing: border-box;
}
.img-container {
float: left;
width: 33.33%;
padding: 5px;
}
.clearfix::after {
content: "";
clear: both;
display: table;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h2>Images Side by Side</h2>
<div class="clearfix">
<div class="img-container">
<img src="1.jpg" style="width:100%">
</div>
<div class="img-container">
<img src="2.jpg" style="width:100%">
</div>
<div class="img-container">
<img src="3.jpg" style="width:100%">
</div>
</div>
</body>
</html>

background-color: white;
min-width: 160px;
box-shadow: 0px 5px 10px 0px;
z-index: 1;
color:red;
}
.dropdown-content a {
color: black;
padding: 12px 16px;
text-decoration: none;
display: block;
text-align: left;
}
.dropdown-content a:hover {background-color: #a1a1a1;}
.dropdown:hover .dropdown-content {
display: block;
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<ul>
<li><a href="#home">Home</a></li>
<li><a href="#about">About Us</a></li>
<li class="dropdown">
<a href="javascript:void(0)" class="dropbtn">News</a>
<div class="dropdown-content">
<a href="#">Hollywood</a>
<a href="#">Bollywood</a>
<a href="#">Kollywood</a>
</div>
</li>
</ul>
</body>
</html>
Output:

CHAPTER 10
INTRODUCTION TO JAVASCRIPT
Introduction: What is scripting language?
It is a list of commands that are embedded in a web page, interpreted and executed by a certain scripting engine.
Scripts are written for various purposes such as automating the processes on a local-computer or to generate web pages on the web. The programming languages in which scripts are written are called scripting languages. Most of the common types of scripting languages are, JavaScript, VBScript, ASP (Active Server Pages), PHP (Hypertext Preprocessor), PERL (Practical Extraction and Reporting Language), JSP (Java Server Pages) etc.
Scripting language is classified in to two types,
-          Client-side script
-          Server-side script
Client-side script: It enables interaction with the webpage. The client-side scripts are downloaded at the client end and then interpreted and executed by the browser. Note that the client-side scripting is browser dependent.
Some sample uses of client-side scripting is listed as below,
-          To get data from users’ screen interface or web browser.
-          Used in online games.
-          Webpage can be customized without reloading the page.
-          In the user login page, if the credentials of the user are incorrect, the webpage shows an error message on the client computer without submitting it to the server.
Server-side script: It enables the completion or carrying out a task at the server end and then sending the result to the client end. In server-side script, the server does all the tasks, so it doesn’t matter which browser is being used at client end. Server-side scripting is used to send the information to the server to process the script at server end.
Some sample uses of server-side scripting is listed as below,
-          Password protection: whenever the user signs up in any webpage, the user name and the password will be saved in the server. Whenever a user wishes to login, the server checks the credentials (username and password) of the user in the server, if it matches then only the user will be allowed to access the server-side resources otherwise, client-side scripting will hold the user back.
-          Browser customization: browser appearance such as change of browser skins and users’ experience when the user is logged into the server with the specific required credentials. Browsers appear different for different users.
-          Form processing: whenever we submit an online application, the data inputted in the form will be stored in the database of the server.
-          Building and displaying pages created from a database: once the data is successfully submitted to the server through online forms, data from the server can be accessed and represented in the form of report, data pages, forms etc.
-          Dynamically editing, changing or adding content to web pages: at server side at any moment we can edit, add, remove or update content of the webpage.
This book is dedicated to JavaScript; hence, we will discuss the JavaScripting language.
JavaScript is a popular, easy to learn web-based programming language. It is also used with HTML and CSS to create responsive web pages and websites. JavaScript was developed by Brendan Eich in 1995. Java and JavaScript both are different languages in concept and design.
JavaScript can change the HTML content using one of the HTML methods getElementById(). Let’s take an example to demonstrate the use of this method.
<html>
<head><title>Changing HTML Content Using JavaScript</title></head>
<body>
<p id="sample">JavaScript can change HTML content.</p>
<button type="button" onclick='document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = "Welcome to the world of JavaScript!">Click Me For Surprise!</button>
</body>
</html>
In this example, an HTML element with id=”sample” and changes the element content innerHTML to "Welcome to the world of JavaScript!"
Output:
Once you click on Click Me For Surprise! Button the content in the HTML changes and looks as shown in the following figure.
Note: JavaScript works fine with both double and single quotes.
JavaScript can change HTML attribute values. We can change the attributes of an HTML code; in the following example we will change the src attribute value and change the image.
<html>
<head><title>JavaScript can change HTML attribute values</title></head>
<body>
<p>JavaScript can change HTML attribute values.</p>
<button onclick="document.getElementById('myImage').src='redcar.jpg'">Change the Image</button>
<img id="myImage" src="bluecar.jpg" style="width:100px">
</body>
</html>
Output:
Upon clicking on change the image button we get the following output.
JavaScript can change HTML CSS styles: The following example demonstrates the use of JavaScript can change HTML and CSS styles.
<html>
<head><title>JavaScript can change HTML CSS styles</title></head>
<body>
<p id="sample">JavaScript can change the style of an HTML element.</p>
<button type="button" onclick="document.getElementById('sample').style.color='red'">Change Color</button>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Upon clicking the Change Color button, JavaScript can change the style of an HTML element text color changes to red as shown in the following output.
JavaScript can hide HTML elements: The property display: none is set to hide an HTML element as shown in the following example,
<html>
<head><title>JavaScript can hide HTML elements</title></head>
<body>
<p id="sample">Enter the Name:<input type="text"></p>
<button type="button" onclick="document.getElementById('sample').style.display='none'">Click Me!</button>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Upon clicking on Click Me! Button, the text box can be hidden as shown in the following output.
JavaScript can show HTML elements: The property display: block is set to show an HTML element as shown in the following example,
<html>
<head><title>JavaScript can show HTML elements</title></head>
<body>
<p id="sample" style="display:none">Enter the Name:<input type="text"></p>
<button type="button" onclick="document.getElementById('sample').style.display='block'">Show Me!</button>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Upon clicking on Show Me! Button, we will be able to see the input box as shown in the following figure.

<html>

</body>

JavaScript function: it is a finite set of lines of code, that will be executed when it is called. These functions will execute when an event occurs, such as button click event, mouse move event, mouse hover event and so on and so forth.
JavaScript can be used in the <BODY> or in the <HEAD> or in both as shown in the following examples given below.
<html>
<head><title>JavaScript in Head and Body Demo</title>
<script>
function myFunction1() {
document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = "Paragraph changed in head.";
}
</script>
</head>
<body>
<p id="sample">A Paragraph.</p>
<script>
function myFunction2() {
document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = "Paragraph changed in body.";
}
</script>
<h2>Demo JavaScript in Head</h2>
<button type="button" onclick="myFunction1()">Check It</button>
<h2>Demo JavaScript in Body</h2>
<button type="button" onclick="myFunction2()">Check It</button>
</body>
</html>
Output:
JavaScript can also be kept in an external file as shown in the following example.
function myFunction() {
document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = "Text Changed.";
}
This function in another notepad saves it with .js extension. This code is written in an external file ExternalScript.js file and can be used in many different webpages. To import this file into the JavaScript code, write the name of the script file in the src attribute of a <SCRIPT> tag as shown in the following example.
<html>
<head><title>External JavaScript Demo</title></head>
<body>
<h2>Demo External JavaScript</h2>
<p id="sample">A Text.</p>
<button type="button" onclick="myFunction()">Check it</button>
<script src="ExternalScript.js"></script>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Upon clicking on the Check it button, it gives the following output as shown below.
Note: You can place an external script reference either in <HEAD> or <BODY> tag and the external scripts cannot contain <SCRIPT> tags as shown in the above example.
