If we enter other than numbers, we get the following result.

CHAPTER 18
SELECTION AND ITERATIVE STATEMENTS
Introduction: In general instructions written are executed in a linear fashion called sequential order. It is not mandatory that all the instructions are to be executed in the order in which they are written, in some situations, the control may bifurcate based on the condition, some statements get executed repeatedly a certain number of times based on the condition. Some statements may jump from one statement to another based on the condition imposed.
JavaScript supports both selection statements and iterative statements.
selection statements: selection statements are also called conditional control statements used to check the condition, based on the condition, the control makes decision and branching.
The selection statements are classified as,
-          if-statement
-          if-else statement
-          if-else-if statement
-          switch statement
if statement: if statement is also called a one-way branching statement. In simple if statements, if the condition is true then the statement or set of statements will get executed. If the condition is false then the next statements, if any, will get executed.
The general form of if statement is,
if(condition)​​OR​​if(condition)
statement1;​​{
​statement1;
​statement2;
​…
​statement-n
​}
Example:
<html>
<head><title>if statement demo</title></head>
<body>
<h2>JavaScript if</h2>
<p id="sample">
<script>
let a=10,b=20,large=a;
if (b > large){
large=b;
document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = "Largest Number is " + large;
}
</script>
</body>
</html>
Output:
if-else statement: if-else statement is also called two-way branching. In an if-else statement the condition will be checked, if the condition is true then statement 1 will get executed. Otherwise, else part i.e. statement 2 will get executed.
The general form of if-else statement is,
if(condition)​
statement 1;
else
statement 2;
Example: Code to find largest of two numbers using if-else statement
<html>
<head><title>if-else statement demo</title></head>
<body>
<h2>JavaScript if-else</h2>
<p id="sample">
<script>
let a=10,b=20;
if (a > b){
document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = "Largest Number is " + a;
}
else
{
document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = "Largest Number is " + b;
}
</script>
</body>
</html>
Output:
if-else-if statement: this statement is also called an if-else-if ladder. There are several alternatives available, out of which we have to choose the statement that matches with the condition. It is also called multiway branching.
The general form of if-else-if statement is,
if(condition1)
​statement1;
else
​if(condition2)
​statement2;
​else
​----
​else
if(condition-n)
​statement-n;
​else
​default statement;
First condition 1 is tested. If the condition is true then statement 1 is executed. Otherwise, condition 2 is tested. If condition 2 is true then statement 2 is executed. Otherwise, condition 3 is tested and so on. Finally, condition-n is tested. If it is true, statement-n is executed. If none of the conditions is true then the default statement is executed.
Example: The following code declares the class depending on percentage.
<html>
<head><title>if-else-if demo</title></head>
<body>
<h2>if-else-if demo</h2>
<p>Input a percentage and click the button:</p>
<input id="percentage" value="" />
<button onclick="perFunction()">Declare Class</button>
<p id="distinction"></p>
<p id="firstclass"></p>
<p id="secondclass"></p>
<p id="passclass"></p>
<p id="fail"></p>
<script>
function perFunction() {
let per = document.getElementById("percentage").value;
if(per>=85&&per<=100)
document.getElementById("distinction").innerHTML = "Distinction";
else
if(per>=60&&per<85)
document.getElementById("firstclass").innerHTML = "First Class";
else
if(per>=50&&per<60)
document.getElementById("secondclass").innerHTML = "Second Class";
else
if(per>=35&&per<50)
document.getElementById("passclass").innerHTML = "Pass Class";
else
document.getElementById("fail").innerHTML = "Fail";
}
</script>
</body>
</html>
Output:
switch statement: it is an alternative of if-else-if ladder. It is used to select one block of code if the expression present in the switch statement matches with the case label. Here switch expression will hold only one of the three values, int, char or Boolean values. This statement provides multiple way branching.
The general form of switch statement is,
switch(expression)
{
​case label-1: statement 1;
​break;
​case label-2: statement 2;
​break;
​…
​case label-n: statement n;
​break;
​default: default-statement;
}
The break statement is used to indicate that the case label is terminated. The default statement will get executed if the switch expression does not match with any case label.
Example: The following code displays the current weekday.
<html>
<head><title>switch statement demo</title></head>
<body>
<h2>switch statement demo</h2>
<p id="sample"></p>
<script>
let day;
switch (new Date().getDay()) {
  case 0:
    day = "Sunday";
    break;
  case 1:
    day = "Monday";
    break;
  case 2:
    day = "Tuesday";
    break;
  case 3:
    day = "Wednesday";
    break;
  case 4:
    day = "Thursday";
    break;
  case 5:
    day = "Friday";
    break;
  case  6:
    day = "Saturday";
}
document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = "Today is " + day;
</script>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Iterative statements: iterative statements that allow repeated execution of a set of instructions till a certain condition is satisfied. Iterative statements are also called looping statements.
Types of loops:
-          for loop – loops through a block of code a number of times.
-          for/in – loops through the properties of an object.
-          for/of – loops through the values of an iterable object.
-          while – loops through a block of code while a specified condition is true.
-          do/while – loops through a block of code while a specified condition is true.
for loop: this looping statement is used when there are a fixed number of iterations. When we know in advance exactly how many times a set of statements should be repeatedly executed.
The general form of for loop is,
for(initialization; condition; increment/decrement)
{
Statement1;
Statement2;
…
Statement-n;
}
Example: The following code to display natural numbers.
<html>
<head><title>for loop demo</title></head>
<body>
<h2>JavaScript For Loop</h2>
<p>Natural Numbers</p>
<p id="sample"></p>
<script>
let str="";
for (let i = 1; i <= 5; i++) {
document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str+=i+"<br>";
}
</script>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Loop scope: Using var in for loop gives the following result.
<html>
<head><title>Using var in for loop</title></head>
<body>
<h2>Using var in for loop</h2>
<p id="sample"></p>
<script>
var i = 5;
for (var i = 0; i < 10; i++) {
  // some statements
}
document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = i;
</script>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Note: Using var the variable declared in the loop redeclares the variable outside the loop.
Using let in for loop gives the following result.
<html>
<head><title>Using let in for loop</title></head>
<body>
<h2>Using let in for loop</h2>
<p id="sample"></p>
<script>
let i = 5;
for (let i = 0; i < 10; i++) {
  // some statements
}
document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = i;
</script>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Note: Using let, the variable declared in the loop does not redeclare the variable outside the loop. When let is used to declare the i variable in a loop, the i variable will be visible within the loop.
The for in loop: This statement loops through the properties of an object.
Syntax:
for (key in object) {
  // code block to be executed
}
Example:
<html>
<head><title>for in loop demo</title></head>
<body>
<h2>For In Loop</h2>
<p id="sample"></p>
<script>
const family_member = {fname:"Hanumanth", lname:"Ladwa", age:38};
let str = "";
for (let a in family_member) {
  str += family_member[a] + " ";
}
document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str;
</script>
</body>
</html>
Output:
for in over arrays: for in statement can also loop over properties of an array.
Syntax:
for (variable in array) {
  code
}
Example:
<html>
<head><title>for in loop over arrays</title></head>
<body>
<h2>JavaScript For In</h2>
<p id="sample"></p>
<script>
const a = [10, 20, 30, 40, 50];
let str = "";
for (let p in a) {
str += a[p] + "<br>";
}
document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str;
</script>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Note: It is better to use a for loop, a for of loop, or Array.forEach() when the index order is important.
Array.forEach() method: This method calls a function once for each array element.
Example:
<html>
<head><title>Array.forEach() demo</title></head>
<body>
<h2>JavaScript Array.forEach()</h2>
<p id="sample"></p>
<script>
const a = [10, 20, 30, 40, 50];
let str = "";
a.forEach(arrayFunction);
document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str;
function arrayFunction(value, index, array) {
str += value + "<br>";
}
</script>
</body>
</html>
Output:
The for of loop: This looping statement loops through the values of an iterable object.
Syntax:
for (variable of iterable) {
  // code block to be executed
}
Here, a variable is used for every iteration the value of the next property is assigned to the variable. Variables can be declared with const, let or var. iterable means, an object that has iterable properties.
Looping over an array:
Example:
<html>
<head><title>for of loop demo</title></head>
<body>
<h2>JavaScript For Of Loop</h2>
<p id="sample"></p>
<script>
const flowers = ["Rose", "Jasmin", "Lilly"];
let str = "";
for (let a of flowers) {
  str += a + "<br>";
}
document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str;
</script>
</body>
</html>
Output:
while loop: it is also called a pretested looping structure, because the condition is checked before repeated execution of a set of statements.
General form,
while(condition)​​OR​​while(condition)
statement;​​{
​statement 1;
​statement 2;
​…
​statement n;
​}
Example: JavaScript code that prints the first 5 even numbers using a while loop.
<html>
<head><title>while loop demo</title></head>
<body>
<h2>JavaScript For Loop</h2>
<p>Even Numbers</p>
<p id="sample"></p>
<script>
let str="";
let i=2;
while (i <= 10) {
if(i%2==0)
document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str+=i+"<br>";
i=i+2;
}
</script>
</body>
</html>
Output:
do-while loop: in this looping structure the condition will be checked executing a set of instructions at least once, hence it is also called a post-tested looping structure.
General form of do-while loop is,
do​​do
{​​{
Statement1;​​OR​​statement 1;
}while(condition);​​statement 2;
​…
​Statement n;
​}while(condition);
Example: JavaScript code to find the sum of first 5 odd numbers.
<html>
<head><title>do while loop demo</title></head>
<body>
<h2>JavaScript do while Loop</h2>
<p>Sum of first five odd Numbers</p>
<p id="sample"></p>
<script>
let i=1,sum=0;
while (i <= 10) {
if(i%2!=0)
document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = sum+=i;
i=i+2;
}
</script>
</body>
</html>
Output:
break statement: break statement can be used in two ways; one we have already used in switch statements to transfer the control out of switch statements. Another is used to terminate the loop.
General form of break statement is,
break;
Example: JavaScript code that breaks out of the for loop using a break statement.
<html>
<head><title>break statement demo</title></head>
<body>
<h2>break statement demo</h2>
<p>Natural Numbers</p>
<p id="sample"></p>
<script>
let str="";
for (let i = 1; i <= 5; i++) {
if(i==3)
break;
document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str+=i+"<br>";
}
</script>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Here, the break statement ends the loop when the loop index i is 3.
continue statement: continue statement is used to skip the part of a looping statement and does not terminate the loop.
The general form of continue statement is,
continue;
Example:
<html>
<head><title>continue statement demo</title></head>
<body>
<h2>continue statement demo</h2>
<p>Natural Numbers</p>
<p id="sample"></p>
<script>
let str="";
for (let i = 1; i <= 5; i++) {
if(i==3)
continue;
document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str+=i+"<br>";
}
</script>
</body>
</html>
Output:
This example skips the value of 3.
Labels: labels and colon are preceded by the statements.
Syntax:
label:statements;
The break and continue statements are the statements that can jump out of a block.
Syntax:
break labelname;
continue label name;
The continue statement with or without a label reference can only be used to skip one loop iteration.
The break statement, without a label reference, can be used to jump out of a loop or a switch statement. With a label reference, the break statement can be used to jump out of any code block.
Example:
<html>
<head><title>break label demo</title></head>
<body>
<h2>JavaScript break label</h2>
<p id="sample"></p>
<script>
const flowers = ["Rose", "Jasmin", "Lilly", "Sunflower"];
let str = "";
list: {
str += flowers[0] + "<br>";
str += flowers[1] + "<br>";
  break list;
str += flowers[2] + "<br>";
str += flowers[3] + "<br>";
}
document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str;
</script>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Sets: A JavaScript set is a collection of unique values and each value can have its occurrence only at once.
Some of the set methods are listed in the following table.
Method
Description
new Set()
Creates a new Set
add()
Adds a new element to the Set
delete()
Removes an element from a Set
has()
Returns true if a value exists in the Set
forEach()
Invokes a callback for each element in the Set
values()
Returns an iterator with all the values in a Set
Property
Description
size
Returns the number elements in a Set
Creating a set: A set can be created by using the new Set() method. And use the add() method to insert values and variables into the set.

Maps: maps can be any data type that holds key values pairs and it remembers the original insertion order of the keys.
Some of the map methods are as listed in the following table.
Method
Description
new Map()
Creates a new Map
set()
Sets the value for a key in a Map
get()
Gets the value for a key in a Map
delete()
Removes a Map element specified by the key
has()
Returns true if a key exists in a Map
forEach()
Calls a function for each key/value pair in a Map
entries()
Returns an iterator with the [key, value] pairs in a Map
Property
Description
size
Returns the number of elements in Map
Map can be created by passing an array to the new Map() method or creating a Map and using Map set() method as shown below.

<script>
const flowers = new Map([
["Rose", 60],
["Jasmin", 20],
["Lilly", 50]
]);
document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = flowers.size;
</script>
</body>

The forEach() method: This method calls a function for each key or value pair in a Map as shown in the following example.
<html>
<head><title>Map objects using forEach() method</title></head>
<body>
<h2>Map objects using forEach() method</h2>
<p id="sample"></p>

const flowers = new Map([
["Rose", 60],
["Jasmin", 20],
["Lilly", 50]
]);
let str="";
flowers.forEach(function(value,key)
{

}
)
document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str;
</script>
</body>

Output:
The entries() method: This method returns an iterator object with the [key,values] in a Map as shown in the following example.
<html>
<head><title>Map objects using entries() method</title></head>
<body>
<h2>Map objects using entries() method</h2>
<p id="sample"></p>
<script>
const flowers = new Map([
["Rose", 60],
["Jasmin", 20],
["Lilly", 50]
]);
let str="";
for(const a of flowers.entries())
{
str += a + "<br>";
}
document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str;
</script>
</body>
</html>
Output:
type conversion: it is a process of converting one type of data into another type. The conversion takes place by using the JavaScript function or automatically by JavaScript itself. JavaScript supports the following conversions.
-          Converting Strings to Number
-          Converting Number to Strings
-          Converting Dates to Number
-          Converting Numbers to Dates
-          Converting Booleans to Numbers
-          Converting Numbers to Booleans
Converting Strings to Numbers: The method Number() can convert strings to numbers. Example, strings containing numbers like “1.25” convert to numbers like 1.25 and empty strings convert to 0 and anything else converts to NaN i.e., Not a Number.
Number methods: The following table lists the number methods.
Method
Description
Number()
Returns a number, converted from its argument
parseFloat()
Parses a string and returns a floating point number
parseInt()
Parses a string and returns an integer
The unary + operator: Can be used to convert a variable to a number as shown in the following example.
<html>
<head><title>typeof operator using + operator demo</title></head>
<body>
<h2>The JavaScript typeof Operator</h2>
<p id="sample"></p>
<script>
let a = "10";
let b = + a;
document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = typeof a + "<br>" + typeof b;
</script>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Note: if the variable cannot be converted, it will still be a number, but with the value NaN.
Converting numbers to strings: The String() method is used to convert numbers to strings as shown in the following example.
<html>
<head><title>String() method demo</title></head>
<body>
<h2>The JavaScript String() Method</h2>
<p id="sample"></p>
<script>
let a = 456;
document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML =
  String(a) + "<br>" +
  String(456) + "<br>" +
  String(450 + 6);
</script>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Note: The toString() method does the same.
Some more methods are listed in the table below.
Method
Description
toExponential()
Returns a string, with a number rounded and written using exponential notation.
toFixed()
Returns a string, with a number rounded and written with a specified number of decimals.
toPrecision()
Returns a string, with a number written with a specified length
Converting dates to numbers: The Number() method is used to convert dates to numbers as shown in the following example.
d = new Date();
Number(d)          // returns 1404568027739
The date method getTime() does the same.
Converting dates to strings: The String() method and toString() method can convert dates to strings.
Example.
String(Date())
Date().toString()
Some of the methods that are used to convert dates to strings are listed in the table below.
Method
Description
getDate()
Get the day as a number (1-31)
getDay()
Get the weekday a number (0-6)
getFullYear()
Get the four digit year (yyyy)
getHours()
Get the hour (0-23)
getMilliseconds()
Get the milliseconds (0-999)
getMinutes()
Get the minutes (0-59)
getMonth()
Get the month (0-11)
getSeconds()
Get the seconds (0-59)
getTime()
Get the time (milliseconds since January 1, 1970)
Converting Booleans to numbers: The number() method can also be used to convert Booleans to numbers.
Example:
Number(false)​// returns 0
Number(true)​// returns 1
Converting Booleans to strings: The String() and toString() methods are used to convert Booleans to strings.
String(false)​// returns “false”
String(true)​// returns “true”
false.toString()​// returns “false”
true.toString()​// returns “true”
Automatic type conversion: JavaScript tries to convert to a suitable type automatically as shown in the following example.
<html>

document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML =
(10 + null) + "<br>"  +
("10" + null) + "<br>" +
("10" + 5) + "<br>" +
("10" - 5) + "<br>" +
("10" * "5") + "<br>" +
("10" / "5") + "<br>"
</script>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Regular expression: It is a sequence of characters that forms a search pattern. When we search for data in a text, we can use this search pattern to describe what you are searching for. Regular expressions can be used to perform text search and text replace operations.
Syntax:
/pattern/modifiers;
Example:
/Hanumanth/i;
Where, Hanumanth is a pattern and ‘i’ is a case insensitive modifier.
The search() and replace() methods are used with regular expression.
The search() method: This method is used to search a string for a specified value and returns the position of the match.
Example:
<html>
<head><title>The search() method demo</title></head>
<body>
<h2>The search() method demo</h2>
<p id="sample"></p>
<script>
let str = "Hanumanth Ladwa";
let n = str.search("Ladwa");
document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = n;
</script>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Note: If we want to use a regular expression with case insensitive search, we use /ladwa/i
The replace() method: This method replaces a specified value with another value in a string as shown in the following example.
<html>
<head><title>The replace() method demo</title></head>
<body>
<h2>The replace() method demo</h2>
<p id="sample">Kindly read my Book</p>
<script>
let str = document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML;
document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML=str.replace("Book", "Bio");
</script>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Note: If we want to use a regular expression with case insensitive replace() method, we use /book/i
Regular expression modifiers: These are listed in the table below.
Modifier
Description
i
Perform case-insensitive matching
g
Perform a global match (find all matches rather than stopping after the first match)
m
Perform multiline matching
Regular expression patterns: brackets are used to find a range of characters.
Expression
Description
[abc]
Find any of the characters between the brackets
[0-9]
Find any of the digits between the brackets
(x|y)
Find any of the alternatives separated with |
Metacharacters are characters with a special meaning as shown in the following table.
Metacharacter
Description
\d
Find a digit
\s
Find a whitespace character
\b
Find a match at the beginning of a word like this: \bWORD, or at the end of a word like this: WORD\b
\uxxxx
Find the Unicode character specified by the hexadecimal number xxxx
Quantifiers are used to define quantities as given in the table below.
Quantifier
Description
n+
Matches any string that contains at least one n
n*
Matches any string that contains zero or more occurrences of n
n?
Matches any string that contains zero or one occurrences of n
The RegExp object: It is a regular expression object with predefined properties and methods.
The test() method: This method is used to search a string for a pattern and returns either true or false value, depending on the result. Let’s look at the example.
<html>
<head><title>The test() method</title></head>
<body>
<h2>The test() method</h2>
<p id="sample1">The sun rises in the east</p>
<p id="sample"></p>
<script>
let str = document.getElementById("sample1").innerHTML;
const pattern = /u/;
document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = pattern.test(str);
</script>
</body>
</html>
Output:
The exec() method: This method searches a string for a specified pattern, and returns the found text as an object. If no match is found, it returns a null. Let’s have an example.
<html>
<head><title>The exec() method</title></head>
<body>
<h2>The exec() method</h2>
<p id="sample"></p>
<script>
const obj = /e/.exec("The sun rises in the east");
document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML =
"Found " + obj[0] + " in position " + obj.index + " in the text: " + obj.input;
</script>
</body>
</html>
Output:

CHAPTER 19
EXCEPTION HANDLING
Introduction: it is very common to make mistakes while writing any computer programs. Such mistakes made by the programmer are called errors. If there are errors present in the program, the program may produce an unintended result. Sometimes programs terminate abruptly. It is always true that, whenever we throw a garbage, we get garbage i.e., garbage in and garbage out (GIGO). If the input is not proper then the output is also not proper.
In order to handle errors or mistakes in a JavaScript program we use error handling keywords as given below.
-          try – this statement is used to monitor the statement or set of statements for errors.
-          catch – this statement is used to handle the error.
-          throw – this statement is used to create custom errors, which creates an error object and throws it whenever error occurs.
-          finally – this statement is used to execute a statement or set of statements even though there are errors in the code. The finally statement will definitely execute.
Errors may be generated due to mistakes while coding, misspelled statements by the programmer or not following the syntax of the programming language.
In the example below, we intentionally typed allert instead of alert, it is misspelled. This type of mistakes can be handled by the try block.
<html>
<head><title>The Error handling</title></head>
<body>
<h2>JavaScript Error Handling</h2>
<p id="sample"></p>
<script>
try {
  allert("Hi there!");
}
catch(e) {
  document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = e.message;
}
</script>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Note: JavaScript catches allert as an error and catch block will handle it.
The try and catch block: The try statement is used to test for errors while it is being executed. The catch statement is used to execute a block of code if there is an error in the try block.
Syntax:
try
{
set of statements to be monitored for errors;
}
catch(e)
{
Set of statements that handle error;
}
Throws errors: It is a process in which the custom error object is created and thrown. JavaScript exceptions could be String, a Number, a Boolean or an Object.
Example:
throw “Text message”;​// this throws a text exception
throw 0;​// this throws a Boolean exception
Example to demonstrate custom exceptions that validates input.
<html>
<head><title>Custom Exception</title></head>
<body>
<p>Please input marks between 0 and 100:</p>
<input id="sample" type="text">
<button type="button" onclick="customFunction()">Check Marks</button>
<p id="sample1"></p>
<script>
function customFunction() {
  const msg = document.getElementById("sample1");
  msg.innerHTML = "";
  let a = document.getElementById("sample").value;
  try {
    if(a == "") throw "empty";
    if(isNaN(a)) throw "not a number";
    a = Number(a);
    if(a < 0) throw "Negative marking not allowed";
    if(a > 100) throw "Marks crossing 100";
  }
  catch(e) {
    msg.innerHTML = "Input is " + e;
  }
}
</script>
</body>
</html>
Output:
The finally statement: This statement will definitely execute even though there is an error in the try block.
Syntax:
try
{
set of statements to be monitored for errors;
}
catch(e)
{
Set of statements that handle error;
}
finally
{
Set of statements that are to be executed regardless of the try and catch block;
}
Example:
<html>
<head><title>The finally block demo</title></head>
<body>
<p>Please input marks between 0 and 100:</p>
<input id="sample" type="text">
<button type="button" onclick="customFunction()">Check Marks</button>
<p id="sample1"></p>
<script>
function customFunction() {
  const msg = document.getElementById("sample1");
  msg.innerHTML = "";
  let a = document.getElementById("sample").value;
  try {
    if(a == "") throw "empty";
    if(isNaN(a)) throw "not a number";
    a = Number(a);
    if(a < 0) throw "Negative marking not allowed";
    if(a > 100) throw "Marks crossing 100";
  }
  catch(e) {
    msg.innerHTML = "Input is " + e;
  }
finally{
document.getElementById("sample").value="";
}
}
</script>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Upon clicking on the Check Marks button, we get the following result.
The error object: When error occurs, JavaScript throws a built in error object. This object has two properties namely, name and message.
The following table lists the error name values.
Error Name
Description
EvalError
An error has occurred in the eval() function
RangeError
A number "out of range" has occurred
ReferenceError
An illegal reference has occurred
SyntaxError
A syntax error has occurred
TypeError
A type error has occurred
URIError
An error in encodeURI() has occurred
The EvalError: This error indicates an error in the eval() function.
The RangeError: This error is thrown when a number lies outside the range of values.
Example:
<html>
<head><title>The RangeError demo</title></head>
<body>
<h2>The RangeError demo</h2>
<p id="sample">
<script>
let n = 1;
try {
  n.toPrecision(500);
}
catch(e) {
  document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = e.name;
}
</script>
</body>
</html>
Output:

<p id="sample">

try {
n=a+1;
}
catch(e) {
  document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = e.name;
}
</script>
</body>
</html>

Example:

CHAPTER 20
CLASSES
Introduction: JavaScript classes are templates for JavaScript objects. Use the keyword class to create a class and always add a method called constructor() as shown in the example below.
class class_name
{
constructor()
{
…
}
}
Example:
class add{
constructor(a,b){
this.a=a;
this.b=b;
}
}
Note: if the variables declared and the parameter names are the same, we use this keyword in order to distinguish between the two.
Example code to demonstrate the use of class.
<html>
<head><title>The class demo</title></head>
<body>
<h2>JavaScript Class</h2>
<p id="sample"></p>
<script>
class add {
  constructor(a, b) {
    this.a = a;
    this.b = b;
  }
}
const sum = new add(10, 20);
document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML =
sum.a + sum.b;
</script>
</body>
</html>
Output:
Constructor: A constructor method is used to initialize object properties. It has the same name as that of the class name. constructors execute automatically as soon as the object is created.
Class methods: When creating a class add a constructor method then add any number of methods as shown in the following syntax:
class class_name{
constructor(){ … }
method1() { … }
method2() { … }
method3() { … }
}
Example:
<html>
<head><title>The class method demo</title></head>
<body>
<h2>The class method demo</h2>
<p id="sample"></p>
<script>
class add {
  constructor(a, b) {
    this.a = a;
    this.b = b;
  }
sum(){
return sum.a + sum.b;
}
}
const sum = new add(10, 20);
document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML =sum.sum();
</script>
</body>
</html>
Output:
JSON: JSON stands for JavaScript Object Notation. This is a format for storing and transporting data. It is often used when data is sent from a server to a webpage. It is a lightweight data interchange format. It is language independent.
Example:
{
"family_members":[
  {"firstName":"Priyanka", "lastName":"Niranjan"},
  {"firstName":"Sneha", "lastName":"Niranjan"},
  {"firstName":"Swaroop", "lastName":"Niranjan"}
]
}
Note that, in JSON code, a name/value pair consists of a property name in double quotations, followed by a colon, followed by a value. JSON objects are written inside curly braces. JSON arrays are written in square brackets.
Converting a JSON text to a JavaScript object: To convert JSON text to a JavaScript object we use JSON.parse() method as shown in the following example.
let str=’{
"family_members":[
  {"firstName":"Priyanka", "lastName":"Niranjan"},
  {"firstName":"Sneha", "lastName":"Niranjan"},
  {"firstName":"Swaroop", "lastName":"Niranjan"}
]
}’;
const obj=JSON.parse(str);
Example:
<html>
<head><title>JSON demo</title></head>
<body>
<h2>Create Object from JSON String</h2>
<p id="sample"></p>
<script>
let str = '{"family_members":[' +
'{"firstName":"Priyanka","lastName":"Niranjan" },' +
'{"firstName":"Sneha","lastName":"Niranjan" },' +
'{"firstName":"Swaroop","lastName":"Niranjan" }]}';
const obj = JSON.parse(str);
document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML =
obj.family_members[1].firstName + " " + obj.family_members[1].lastName;
</script>
</body>
</html>
Output:

// 3. Prepare a time table for college.
<html>
<head>
<title>College Time Table</title>
</head>
<body>

// 6. JavaScript code to display all 12 months of a specified year.
<html>
<head>
<script language="javascript">
function day_title(day_name)
{
document.write("<td align=center width=35 bgcolor=lightblue><b>"+day_name+"</b></td>");
}
function fill_table(month,month_len)
{
day=1;
document.write("<table border=1 cellspacing=3 cellpadding=3%>");
document.write("<td colspan=7 align=center bgcolor=red><b>"+month+" "+year+"</b><tr>");
day_title("Sun");
day_title("Mon");
day_title("Tue");
day_title("Wed");
day_title("Thu");
day_title("Fri");
day_title("Sat");
document.write("</tr><tr>");
for(var i=1; i<start_day;i++)
{
document.write("<td>");
}
for(var i=start_day; i<8;i++)
{
document.write("<td align=center bgcolor=silver>"+day+"</td>");
day++;
}
document.write("<tr>");
while(day<=month_len)
{
for(var i=1; i<=7 && day<=month_len;i++)
{
document.write("<td align=center bgcolor=silver>"+day+"</td>");
day++;
}
document.write("<tr>");
start_day=i;
}
document.write("</tr></table><br>");
}
year=prompt("enter 4 digit year ",2016);
today=new Date("January 1, "+year);
start_day=today.getDay()+1;
fill_table("January", 31);
if (year%4==0)
fill_table("February", 29);
else
fill_table("February", 28);
fill_table("March", 31);
fill_table("April", 30);
fill_table("May", 31);
fill_table("June", 30);
fill_table("July", 31);
fill_table("August", 31);
fill_table("September", 30);
fill_table("October", 31);
fill_table("November", 30);
fill_table("December", 31);
</script>
</head>
</html>

Once we type 2021 and click on the OK button, we get the calendar from January 2021 to December 2021 as shown below.
/* 7. JavaScript code that changes the background color of the canvas of the browser window. */
<html>
<head><title>Random color change</title>
<script type="text/javascript">
function get_random_color() {
var color = '#';
for (var i = 0; i < 6; i++ )
{
color += Math.round(Math.random())*6;
}
document.body.style.background= color;
}
</script>
</head>
<Button onclick="get_random_color()">Click me to change the color</Button>
</body>
</html>

<td><input type=button id=b16 value='/' size=2 onclick=f('/')></td>
</tr>

<td colspan=4 ><input type=button value=Clear size=16 onclick=f('c')></td>
</tr>
</table>
</body>
</html>
Save this file with .html extension and the code below save it as javascriptcode.js
function f(d)
{
if(d=='c')
{
document.getElementById('res').value="";
return;
}
res=document.getElementById('res').value;
if(res==0 && d==0)
return;
if(d=='+' || d=='-' || d=='*' || d=='/')
{
opr=d;
num=parseFloat(res);
document.getElementById('res').value=d;
return;
}
if(d=='=')
{
num1=parseFloat(res);
switch(opr)
{
case '+': ans=num+num1; break;
case '-': ans=num-num1; break;
case '*': ans=num*num1; break;
case '/': ans=parseInt(num/num1); break;
}
document.getElementById('res').value=ans;
return;
}
if(d=='--')
{
document.getElementById('res').value*=-1;
return;
}
if(!isNaN(document.getElementById('res').value))
document.getElementById('res').value+=d;
else
document.getElementById('res').value=d;
}

/* 10. JavaScript code to display the content depending on the range selected by the user. */
<html>
<head><title>Selecting table range</title></head>
<script type=text/javascript src=javascriptcode2.js>
</script>
<body onload=sc()>
</body>
</html>
Save this code with .html extension and the code below save it as javascritptcode2.js
function sc()
{
rng=prompt('Enter the range');
res=rng.split("-");
if(res.length!=2)
{
alert("invalid range ");
return;
}
first=parseInt(res[0]);
second=parseInt(res[1]);
if(first>second)
{
alert("invalid range ");
return;
}
str="<table border=2><tr><th>Number</th><th>Square</th><th>Cube</th></tr>";
for(i=first;i<=second;i++)
{
str=str+"<tr><td>"+i+"<td>"+(i*i)+"<td>"+(i*i*i);
}
document.write(str);
}

// 11. JavaScript code to reverse a number.
<html>
<head><title>Reversing a number</title></head>

functions</title> </head>
<script type=text/javascript src=javascriptcode3.js>
</script>
<body>
<section>
<h1>Reverse of a number</h1>
<p>Enter a number: <input type=text id=t2></p>
<input type=button value=Reverse
onclick=alert(reverse(document.getElementById('t2').value))>
</section>
</body>
</html>
Save this file with .html extension and the code below save it as javascriptcode3.js
function reverse(num)
{
rnum=0;
temp=num;
if(isNaN(num))
{
return "invalid number";
}
while(num!=0)
{
rnum *= 10;
rnum+= num % 10;
num -= num % 10;
num = Math.floor(num/10);
}
return "Reverse of num "+ temp + " is " + rnum;
}

// 12. JavaScript code to display numbers in words.
<script language="javascript">
function convert_number_to_words(number)
{
hyphen = '-';
conjunction = ' and ';
separator = ', ';
dictionary = {
0 : 'zero',
1 : 'one',
2 : 'two',
3 : 'three',
4 : 'four',
5 : 'five',
6 : 'six',
7 : 'seven',
8 : 'eight',
9 : 'nine',
10 : 'ten',
11 : 'eleven',
12 : 'twelve',
13 : 'thirteen',
14 : 'fourteen',
15 : 'fifteen',
16 : 'sixteen',
17 : 'seventeen',
18 : 'eighteen',
19 : 'nineteen',
20 : 'twenty',
30 : 'thirty',
40 : 'fourty',
50 : 'fifty',
60 : 'sixty',
70 : 'seventy',
80 : 'eighty',
90 : 'ninety',
100 : 'hundred',
};
if (number < 0 || number > 999)
{
alert("Enter a number range between 0 and 999");
return "";
}
switch (true)
{
case (number < 21):
string = dictionary[number];
break;
case (number < 100):
tens = parseInt(number / 10) * 10;
units = number % 10;
string = dictionary[tens];
if (units) {
string += hyphen + dictionary[units];
}
break;
case (number < 1000):
hundreds = parseInt(number / 100);
remainder = number % 100;
string = dictionary[hundreds] + ' ' + dictionary[100];
if (remainder) {
string += conjunction + convert_number_to_words(remainder);
}
break;
default:
break;
}
return string;
}
a=prompt("Enter a number");
num=parseInt(a);
document.write(convert_number_to_words(num));
</script>
Note: save this code with .html extension

// 14. JavaScript code to find the factorial of a number.
<html>  
<head><title>Factorial of a number</title></head> 
<body>  
Enter a number: <input id = "num"> 
<br><br>  
<button onclick = "fact()"> Factorial </button> 
<p id = "sample"></p> 
<script>  
function fact(){  
var i, num, fact; 
fact = 1; 
num = document.getElementById("num").value; 
for(i = 1; i <= num; i++)   
{  
fact = fact * i; 
}  
i=i-1;
document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = "The factorial of the number " + i + " is: " + fact ; 
}  
</script>  
</body>  
</html>

// 15. JavaScript code for registration form validation.
<html>
<head><title>Registration Form Validation</title></head>
<body bgcolor="#E4F0F8">
<script type='text/javascript'>
function formValidator()
{
var firstname = document.getElementById('firstname');
var lastname = document.getElementById('lastname');
var email = document.getElementById('email');
var pass = document.getElementById('pass');
var addr = document.getElementById('addr');
var mobileno = document.getElementById('mobileno');
// Check each input in the order that it appears in the form!
if(notEmpty(firstname, "can not be null")){
if(isAlphabet(firstname, "Please enter only letters for your Firstname")){
if(lengthRestriction(firstname, 6)){
if(isAlphabet(lastname, "Please enter only letters for your Lastname")){
if(emailValidator(email, "Please enter a valid email address")){
if(lengthRestriction(pass, 6)){
if(isAlphanumeric(pass, "please enter Numbers and Letters Only for password")){
if(notEmpty(addr, "please enter the address")){
if(isNumeric(mobileno, "Please enter a valid mobileno")){
if(lengthRestriction1(mobileno, 10 , 10)){
return true;
} } } }
}
}
}
} } }
return false;
}
function notEmpty(elem, helperMsg){
if(elem.value.length == 0){
alert(helperMsg);
elem.focus(); // set the focus to this input
return false;
}
return true;
}
function isNumeric(elem, helperMsg){
var numericExpression = /^[0-9]+$/;
if(elem.value.match(numericExpression)){
return true;
}else{
alert(helperMsg);
elem.focus();
return false;
}
}
function isAlphabet(elem, helperMsg){
var alphaExp = /^[a-zA-Z]+$/;
if(elem.value.match(alphaExp)){
return true;
}else{
alert(helperMsg);
elem.focus();
return false;
}
}
function isAlphanumeric(elem, helperMsg){
var alphaExp = /^[0-9a-zA-Z]+$/;
if(elem.value.match(alphaExp)){
return true;
}else{
alert(helperMsg);
elem.focus();
return false;
}
}
function lengthRestriction(elem, min){
var uInput = elem.value;
if(uInput.length >= min){
return true;
}else{
alert("Please enter minimum " +min+ " characters");
elem.focus();
return false;
}
}
function emailValidator(elem, helperMsg)
{
var emailExp = /^[\w\-\.\+]+\@[a-zA-Z0-9\.\-]+\.[a-zA-z0-9]{2,4}$/;
if(elem.value.match(emailExp))
{
return true;
}
else{
alert(helperMsg);
elem.focus();
return false;
}
}
function lengthRestriction1(elem, min, max)
{
var uInput = elem.value;
if(uInput.length >= min && uInput.length <= max)
{
return true;
}
else {
alert("Please enter 10 numbers only");
elem.focus();
return false;
}
}
</script>
<center><font color="blue" size="6" face="arial">Registration Form</font></center><br />
<form onsubmit='return formValidator()' action="right.html">
First Name(Minimum 6 characters)<font color="red">* </font>
<input type='text' id='firstname' /><br /><br />
Last Name<font color="red"><font color="red">* </font> </font> &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
<input type='text' id='lastname' /><br /><br />
Email Address<font color="red">* </font> &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
<input type='text' id='email' /><br />
<font color="red">(one e-mail id only):</font> &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
<font color="redblue">e.g. smith@hotmail.com</font><br /><br/>
Password(minimum 6 characters)<font color="red">* </font> &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
<input type='password' id='pass'><br /><br/>
Address<font color="red">* </font> &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
<textarea rows="2" cols="20" id='addr' /></textarea> <br /> <br/>
Mobile No<font color="red">* </font> &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
<input type='text' id='mobileno' /><br />
Gender: <input type='radio' name="gender">male
<input type='radio' name="gender">female<br/><br />
<input type='Submit' value='submit' />
<input type='Reset' value='reset' />
</form>
</body>
</html>
